Nothing is invented, no row is ever out of order, and the head guard (`leave ≤ board₁`, a real impossibility) is untouched, which is the direction real staleness actually travels — swaps, pushes and reroutes all move things LATER. The tail keeps a guard, but a sound one: COHERENCE, elapsed against elapsed (`alight_last − board₁ ≤ arrive − leave`), which fresh data always clears and an inverted window never does. `shouldShowTimeline` was NOT touched — it was never the problem. **DEFECT TWO — THE SWAP ENGINE PRICED THE HOME LEG AT "NOW", AND THIS IS THE ONE THAT MATCHES THE SYMPTOM EXACTLY.** `planAnchorInbound` passes `prevStop.end_time` as the departure for every inbound leg that HAS a previous stop; the home leg has none and passed `undefined`, so `computeRoute` sent no `departureTime` at all and Google answered for the current clock. Proved side by side against the live API: the same home leg priced at the 7pm departure boards at 23:10Z, and priced with no departure boards at **13:33Z — nine and a half hours before you leave**. Those instants are stored on the itinerary, so after any swap touching stop 0 the home leg's board time sits permanently before its leave time, the staleness guard refuses it forever, and the button never comes back — on the FIRST leg and only the first, because every other leg departs at a stored `end_time`. The minutes were wrong too (68 vs 70 on a quiet morning; off-peak against rush hour is worse). `homeDeparture()` is the anchor's start less the stored home leg — the same subtraction the strip already renders as "leave by" — bootstrapping the new leg's departure from the old leg's length exactly as `getTravelLegs` accumulates, since a leg's length cannot be known before it is priced and being minutes out on the timetable you ask for is not the same class of error as being hours out. `anchorStart` is the fallback for a plan with no stored home leg. **`reroute.ts` never had this defect** (it always passes `new Date(cursorMs).toISOString()` and never touches the home leg) and neither did the initial pipeline (`departureTime: startISO`), so this was a one-caller gap, now closed and documented so a third caller does not re-open it. **WHY NO TEST CAUGHT EITHER:** `mockLeg` boards at `departure + MOCK_WALK_TO_STOP_MIN` — it models a ZERO platform wait, which no real agency has, so the fixture can never produce the overflow; and the mock home leg is priced through `mockTravelLegs`, which threads the real departure, so the swap path's `undefined` never reaches it. Both are data properties of the fixture, not gaps in the seam, and neither is worth bending the fixture to fake — `fixtures.spec.ts` pins the leg's minutes and its one-ride line. **TESTS: `transitDetail.test.ts` 29 → 32** (the wait case drops the arrive ROW rather than the timeline and the rows stay ordered; the live 3-ride leg this was found on, transcribed instant for instant from the Routes response, opens AND clears `shouldShowTimeline`; the coherence bound refuses a ride span longer than its leg; an inverted window refuses) and **`swap.test.ts` 79 → 81** (the home leg is priced at 18:40 for a 19:00 anchor and explicitly not `undefined`; a plan with no stored home leg still prices inside the plan). The case that asserted "an alight AFTER you arrive is refused too" was deliberately rewritten — it encoded the bug. **TWO REVERT-RUNS, BOTH DISCRIMINATING:** restoring `arriveMs < previousMs → return null` turned exactly the two WAIT cases red at 30/32 while both coherence cases stayed green (they assert null either way, which is the point of running it); restoring `?? undefined` turned exactly the two home-departure cases red at 79/81. Both restored and re-verified, 32/32 and 81/81. **LIVE-VERIFIED on :3200 against the real APIs (3000 untouched)**, on a real plan from a Bayview Village start into Kensington Market whose home leg is the reported shape — three rides, two transfers, `4 Sheppard → 1 Yonge - University → 505 Dundas` in real line colours. Compact at a quiet hour showing `69 min` and `leave 7:00 PM · arrive 8:09 PM`; tapped, it opens to all eight rows with each badge on its own board row and "SCHEDULED TIMES" beneath; tapped again it closes; with the dev clock moved to 7:30 PM — inside the leg — it opens with NO tap while no stop wears a "now" pill, which is the honest picture of that instant; moved back to noon it closes again. Then the half that used to break it: a venue swap on stop 0, after which the stored home leg still boards at 7:10/7:18/7:48 PM (it would have been ~9:40 AM) and the card still opens. The walking leg is byte-identical and correctly has no button at all. The button measures 178×60, well clear of the 44px floor. **FLAGGED, NOT FIXED — and it is the thing the arrive row is telling you:** when the forecast loses to the published alight, what that really means is the PLAN under-budgeted that leg by the platform wait, so it can hand you an arrival earlier than the train does. Fixing it means putting the wait into `totalMinutes`, which is a scheduling change and the one thing this whole slice forbids (`buildSchedule` runs on `totalMinutes`; a provider board time is a timetable for the instant the leg was priced at, and re-deriving the schedule from it would make a forecast into a commitment). Recorded in CLAUDE.md rather than done quietly.

## Refinement: the route badge moves to where the route is named — inline, in both states of the card
Goal: the leg card carried its coloured route badges STACKED in the top-left corner and then spelled the same routes out again underneath — a "091" circle and a "1" circle above the words "091 Bayview → 1 Yonge - University". The number was printed twice, once as a shape and once as text, and the shape sat nowhere near the words it belonged to; on the expanded breakdown the badges stayed pinned to the corner while the ride they identify was four rows down. A badge is an identifier, so it belongs where its route is referenced.
What should be done: put each badge INLINE — in the collapsed title beside the place that route serves, and in the expanded timeline beside that ride's own board row — delete the corner stack from both states, and stop spelling the route number out in the text the badge now carries. Degrade sensibly by leg shape (transfer / single ride / walk / a line the agency names but does not number), reuse the existing circle rather than build a second one, and touch nothing else: not the parse, not the provider times, not the transfer markers, not the scheduler, not the active-or-selected visibility rule.
What was done (technical): **THE SPLIT IS A PURE FUNCTION AND IT IS THE WHOLE FEATURE.** `linePlace` + `lineBadges` in `transitBubbles.ts` (beside `bubbleLabel`, which they consume rather than duplicate) turn the leg's rides into an ordered `{segment, badge, place}` model: the badge is what the circle prints, the place is the line's published name with the designation the badge already carries taken off the FRONT. Agencies write that designation two ways — "091 Bayview" and "Line 2 Bloor–Danforth" — so it is looked for in the first TWO tokens only; deeper in, a matching token is a coincidence rather than a prefix ("Queen West to 501 Loop" keeps every word). **THE TEST IS WHAT THE CIRCLE ACTUALLY PRINTS, NOT WHAT THE AGENCY PUBLISHED**, and that is what makes the fallbacks safe: an initials badge ("Lakeshore West" → "LW") and a designation clipped to circle width ("EXPRESS" → "EXPR") are not literally in the name, so the name stands WHOLE — exactly what the card printed before this split existed. Cutting a word the badge only half-says would lose it from the card entirely. A name that is nothing BUT the designation ("Line 2") yields a null place and the badge stands alone. **DEGRADE BY LEG SHAPE, all four handled at the same seam:** a TRANSFER is N entries with an arrow between them; a SINGLE ride is one entry and therefore no arrow (the `· 8 stops` suffix still prints, still only when one ride owns the leg); a WALK has no rides, so `lineBadges([])` is `[]` — no badge, the mode glyph and the walk copy untouched; and a leg with no segments at all (an `unknown` estimate, a plan stored before segments existed) takes the same empty path and keeps its plain spelled-out line. Colour is unchanged and still the agency's: `seg.color || var(--ink)` on the same `.lstrip__bubble` base class, so an unpublished colour is an ink circle rather than a broken one. **THE EXPANDED HALF NEEDED ONE FIELD, NOT A NEW MODEL.** `buildTransitTimeline`'s board/alight rows now carry `rideIndex`, so a row finds its OWN badge by position instead of matching on the line's name — a leg can ride the same line twice, and a name match would hand the second ride the first's circle. The timeline row became a three-column grid with a 22px badge RAIL: the badge hangs in it on the board rows (that is where you get ON a route; leave, alight and arrive get an empty cell), which keeps every row's word starting at the same column instead of shifting the ride rows right. **THE FOUR-INSTANT CONTENT IS UNTOUCHED** — same rows, same times, same `line · stop` caption, and the caption still spells the full published name because in the DETAIL view it identifies the ride unambiguously next to its stop; only the badge was added. **LAYOUT: ORDINARY TEXT FLOW, and the first attempt was wrong.** Built first as a wrapping flex row per ride, which reads correctly at any width the mock fixture produces and then breaks "Yonge - Universi/ty" mid-word on a real transfer — a flex row gives the name only the sliver left beside its own circle, in a 118px card that is ~44px. The badge is now an inline element with `vertical-align: middle` in normal text flow, so a long name wraps across the card's FULL width the way the spelled-out line always did; the arrow belongs to the ride it leads into, so a wrap never strands one on its own line. **ACCESSIBILITY IS A TRADE MADE EXPLICITLY:** the circle is `aria-hidden` (it is a colour and a designation) and the line's PUBLISHED name rides beside it in an `sr-only` span, with the visible place text hidden as the duplicate — a screen reader hears "505 Fixture · 8 stops" and "63 Ossington, then Line 2 Bloor–Danforth", byte-identical to what it heard before. The 44px floor is untouched: the badge is decorative, `.lstrip__legselect` is still the target, and the mobile spec's generic `.lstrip button` sweep at 320/375/390/768 passes unchanged. **DEAD CSS DELETED RATHER THAN LEFT:** `.lstrip__bubbles` / `.lstrip__bunit` / `.lstrip__bubble--sm` were the stack's own rules and nothing else used them; the MAP's leg-label pills have their own `leglab__*` classes and `groupBubbleUnits` is still theirs, so the map is byte-identical. **TESTS: `transitBubbles.test.ts` 7 → 14 cases** (the ordered multi-ride model, the single-ride no-arrow case, walk → no badge, the missing-short-name and clipped-badge fallbacks, "the number is never printed twice" across both spellings, the designation-only name, and the front-only rule), **`transitDetail.test.ts` 28 → 29** (every board/alight row names its ride, pinned BY INDEX on a leg that rides the same line twice). `fixtures.spec.ts` gained the browser half — badge "505" inside the collapsed title with the place reading "Fixture", zero `.lstrip__bubbles` anywhere, exactly ONE badge in the expanded timeline and it is on the board row, and no badge on any walking leg — and its two existing pins moved deliberately: the collapsed title's "505 Fixture" now passes through the `sr-only` name rather than visible text, so it is asserted explicitly alongside the badge. **REVERT-RUN, discriminating, both halves:** breaking the pure split (never cut the designation off the name) turned exactly the four split cases red at 10/14 while grouping, labels, the walk case and both fallbacks stayed green; dropping `<RouteBadge>` out of the title turned the e2e red at exactly `.lstrip__legline .lstrip__bubble` ("element(s) not found") with every deterministic-pipeline assertion before it still green. Both restored and re-verified, 14/14 and 29/29. **GATE:** `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors with the same 11 long-standing warnings, typecheck clean, 46 unit suites, webpack build, 81/81 mock e2e including both clock-dependent stated-window cases. **EYEBALLED on a mock production server on :3200** (3000 untouched), and the MULTI-RIDE shape the fixture cannot produce was rendered through a throwaway `/strip-specimen` route — the same precedent the stacked bubbles were designed with, deleted before commit: collapsed reads `[091] Bayview → [1] Yonge - University` in real line colours with no corner stack, expanded puts the 091 circle on the first board row and the yellow 1 on the second, a single-ride leg shows one badge and no stray arrow, an unnumbered line shows an ink `LW` circle with "Lakeshore West" whole beside it, and the walk leg is byte-identical. **NOT COVERED, same wall as the transfer markers:** the mock fixture is deliberately one ride, so the arrow, the second badge and the multi-badge rail have no mock e2e — they need the owner's live pass.

## Refinement: the leg shows its times when you're ON it, or when you ask — and never asks you to press a switch
Goal: the four-instant breakdown shipped in the entry below was right, and its packaging was not. It sat behind a "board times / hide times" disclosure, which is a control the traveller has to notice, decide about and operate for information they have either just asked for or are standing in the middle of; and the summary above it read "1 transfer · 85 min · incl 5 buffer", which spends the card's one quiet line on a transfer count the bubbles already show and a scheduling buffer nobody can act on. What the leg should say at rest is what it IS and how long it takes.
What should be done: delete the toggle and its manual-open gate, cut the summary to the total alone, and let the card decide for itself — full breakdown when the traveller is ON this leg right now OR when they tapped it, compact otherwise. Reuse the app's existing clock and the leg's existing per-card state; add no timer, and touch no scheduling, no parse, no provider-time reading and no transfer markers.
What was done (technical): **THE RULE IS A PURE FUNCTION AND IT IS TWO LINES OF POLICY.** `shouldShowTimeline({isTransit, hasTimeline, isActiveNow, isSelected})` in `transitDetail.ts` (beside `buildTransitTimeline`, which it consumes rather than duplicates) returns `isActiveNow || isSelected` behind the two gates the card always had: only a TRANSIT leg has rides, and only a leg whose provider instants survived the timeline builder — published, complete, still monotone — has anything honest to open into. That ordering is the load-bearing part: **neither being on a leg nor tapping it can conjure times that were never published or that no longer belong to it**, so the single-line fallback and the staleness fallback are reached exactly as before. `legUnderway(leg, nowMs)` is the other new pure function: the leg's window is the SCHEDULER's own two instants (`leaveISO` → `arriveISO`, the pair the entry below named correctly), compared half-open `[leave, arrive)` — the same shape `deriveStopStatus` gives a stop, so the instant you arrive belongs to the venue rather than to the ride. An unreadable window is not underway: a window that cannot be read cannot be shown to contain anything. **"NOW" DOES NOT LIVE-TICK, AND NOTHING WAS BUILT TO MAKE IT.** There is no interval anywhere in the app — stop statuses are derived SERVER-side on each read (`withStatuses`) and the browser re-reads on an action, not on a clock. So the auto-show re-evaluates on the render cadence the app already has (plan load, swap, reroute, selection, a dev-clock change) and not on a timer; a leg that becomes active while nobody touches the page will not open by itself until the next render. That is a real limitation, recorded rather than papered over — the alternative was a `setInterval` in the strip, which is a second clock in an app whose whole time story is "one instant, shared". **THE INSTANT IS THREADED, NOT INVENTED.** `page.tsx` computes `displayNow` at render — the dev sim clock when set, the real clock otherwise, which is exactly the instant the store was asked for when it decided which stop wears the "now" pill — and passes it to the strip as `now`. Reading `Date.now()` inside the card instead would have been fewer lines and would have put the leg check on a different clock from the stop highlight the moment anyone simulated time. Existing `formatStopTime(iso, new Date(), zone)` label calls were deliberately left alone: this change decides VISIBILITY, not what a label says. **THE SELECTION IS THE CARD.** The `open` disclosure state became `selected`, and its trigger is the leg summary itself — a native `<button class="lstrip__legselect">` carrying the glyph, the line and the minutes, with the timeline as its SIBLING, mirroring `.lstrip__select` on the stop card and the invariant that a strip summary is a native selection button with expandable content beside it, never inside it. A transit leg with no usable times is not wrapped in a button at all: a control that cannot do anything is worse than no control. `BubbleStack`'s wrapper divs became spans and `.lstrip__legicon` got `display: block`, so the button's content is phrasing content and nothing moved a pixel. **THE SUMMARY IS THE TOTAL.** `"85 min · incl 5 buffer"` → `"85 min"`; the identity line stays and got MORE honest for a multi-ride leg — `"1 transfer"` (which named a count, not a route, and left the actual lines readable only in the `aria-hidden` bubbles) is now the lines you ride, in order, joined: `"63 Ossington → Line 2 Bloor–Danforth"`. A single-ride leg is unchanged (`"505 Fixture · 8 stops"` — stopCount is the first ride's, so it still prints only when one ride owns it). `StripLeg.marginMinutes` is kept on the view-model though nothing renders it: the buffer is a real fact about the leg, and removing it from the shape would be a data change in a display-only build. **KNOWN AND DELIBERATE:** an ACTIVE leg cannot be tapped shut — the OR makes the clock win — and `aria-expanded` stays `true` so the control never lies about the state; the tap still toggles the underlying selection, which takes effect the moment the leg stops being active. **TESTS: `transitDetail.test.ts` 18 → 28 cases**, the six the rule is specified by (active → full, selected → full, both → full, neither → compact, non-transit → never full at any clock/tap combination, no usable times → never full at any combination) plus the window arithmetic (inside, before, after, both boundaries, unreadable instants, a NaN clock) and one end-to-end case proving a STALE leg still falls back while it is being ridden. `fixtures.spec.ts` now parks the dev clock before asserting: the home leg's window is the sixteen minutes before the first stop, so a real-clock run could have sat inside it and flipped the collapsed-state assertion — the spec sets 12:00 on the plan's day for the tap half (compact → tap → four rows → tap → compact, plus the summary pinned to `/^\d+ min$/` so "buffer" cannot come back), then 19:05 for the other half, which is inside the home leg by construction (it departs at the 19:00 anchor and any transit leg runs at least the 8-minute floor plus its 5-minute margin) and proves the leg opens with NO tap and closes again when the clock moves away. `simAt` moved from `scenarios.spec.ts` into `helpers.ts` (now with minutes) rather than being forked. The mobile spec needed no edit and lost no coverage: its selector is `.lstrip button`, generic, so the new summary button is swept for the 44px floor automatically at 320/375/390/768 — the removed toggle was never named by class anywhere. **REVERT-RUN, discriminating:** breaking the OR to `return isSelected` turned exactly ONE unit case red ("ACTIVE shows the full breakdown with no tap", 27/28) and the e2e red at exactly the auto-show line, with the compact-by-default and tap-to-expand assertions before it still green; restored and re-verified 28/28. **GATE:** `npm run check` green at 01:47 local — lint 0 errors with the same 11 long-standing warnings, typecheck clean, 46 unit suites, webpack build, 81/81 mock e2e including both clock-dependent stated-window cases. **EYEBALLED on a mock server on :3200** (3000 untouched): no toggle and no "board times" string anywhere in the DOM, the summary reads `505 Fixture · 8 stops` / `16 min` / `leave 7:00 PM · arrive 7:16 PM`, tapping the card opens leave 7:00 → board 7:02 → alight 7:10 → arrive 7:16 under SCHEDULED TIMES, tapping again closes it, and setting the dev clock to 7:05 PM opens it with no tap while no stop shows a "now" pill — which is the honest picture of that instant: you are on the train, not at a venue. The walk leg is byte-identical. **NOT COVERED, same wall as the transfer markers:** the multi-ride identity line has no mock e2e, because the fixture is deliberately one ride; it needs the owner's live pass.

## Feature: real per-ride transit times, and a marker where you change lines
Goal: the leg card said "board 7:00 PM · arrive 7:16 PM" and both halves were the scheduler's own numbers — the dwell end and the next stop's start, the padded door-to-door span with the first instant misnamed "board". You do not board the train when you stand up from dinner, and that mislabel is exactly why the leg's numbers looked like they did not add up: the walk to the stop, the wait on the platform and every transfer were folded invisibly inside one span. The real board and alight times were already arriving on every transit response and being thrown away at the parser, along with the coordinates of the stops themselves.
What should be done: read the four facts that already arrive under the current field mask (no mask change, no extra call, no cost), carry them through both validators, show the FOUR instants honestly — leave → board → alight → arrive, per ride — and mark each transfer on the map at its own real coordinate. Touch no scheduling: provider times are display only.
What was done (technical): **THE DATA WAS ALREADY IN THE RESPONSE.** `routes.legs.steps.transitDetails` is a PARENT path in the field mask, so Google returns the whole nested object — the same reason `color`/`vehicle` surfaced for free earlier. `extractTransitSegments` was reading `stopDetails.departureStop.name` and stopping there, discarding `stopDetails.departureTime`/`arrivalTime` and each stop's `location.latLng`. Those four now ride on `TransitSummary` as `boardISO`/`alightISO`/`boardLocation`/`alightLocation`, optional AND nullable in both directions (absent on every plan stored before today, null when the agency publishes none), read through two small guards — an instant is kept VERBATIM only if it parses, a coordinate only if both halves are finite and in range, and half a coordinate is dropped whole because a marker must never sit at a number nobody published. **NOTHING SCHEDULING READS THEM, and that is the load-bearing constraint.** `buildSchedule` still runs on `leg.totalMinutes` (the padded number), `checkWindowFit` is untouched, and every computed time is byte-identical — a test asserts the multi-ride leg's `rawMinutes`/`totalMinutes` are exactly what they were. A provider board time is the timetable for the instant the leg was PRICED at; re-deriving the schedule from it would turn a forecast into a commitment. **BOTH VALIDATORS WERE EXTENDED**, per the project's validate-what-crosses-the-wire posture: `parseTravelLegs` server-side (which, notably, validated nothing about the ride until now) and `isTravelLeg`/`isTransitSummary` in the browser. Present-and-malformed is the only rejection, so a walk leg, an older stored plan and a ride with no published times all still pass — the coordinates especially are checked, since a bad one becomes a map marker. **THE DISPLAY MODEL IS A PURE MODULE, `app/lib/transitDetail.ts`**, shared by the strip and the map so they cannot drift: `buildTransitTimeline` returns leave → board → alight → arrive (a board/alight pair PER RIDE), or NULL meaning "fall back to the single line". **THE STALENESS ANSWER IS MONOTONICITY, and it is the one design decision here worth arguing about.** A swap or reroute re-fetches the legs it moves, but a leg it does NOT move keeps provider times anchored to the old departure while its window shifts underneath it. Rather than invent a tolerance constant, the timeline renders only when leave ≤ board₁ ≤ alight₁ ≤ … ≤ arrive: a plan shifted later leaves a board time BEFORE you leave, one shifted earlier leaves an alight AFTER you arrive, and either way the card silently falls back to the two instants the scheduler owns. Missing instants and PARTIAL multi-ride legs fall back too — a hidden middle ride would still read as a complete journey, which is worse than showing less. What does render is labelled SCHEDULED TIMES, not promised. Recorded rather than hidden: a plan that moved a couple of minutes EARLIER can stay monotone and still be slightly stale; the failure is conservative and a fuzzier rule would cost more than it saves. **THE BUBBLE grew rather than crowded**: collapsed it reads `leave 7:00 PM · arrive 7:16 PM` (the same two instants as before, finally named correctly), with a "board times" disclosure that widens the card 118 → 208px and lists each instant with the line and stop it happens at. The walk leg's branch, including the mandatory beta caution, is untouched; the toggle carries a 44px target at every width (the layout spec sweeps 320/375/390/768 and caught it at 17px on the first pass) and uses `--accent`, never the reserved chartreuse. **TRANSFER MARKERS ARE DRAWN AT A REAL COORDINATE OR NOT AT ALL.** `transferPoints` pairs ride i's alight with ride i+1's board, takes the alight coordinate (where the change begins) with the next ride's board coordinate as the fallback, and the stop NAME always follows whichever coordinate was used so a marker never wears another stop's label. With neither published it emits nothing. Deliberately NOT `midPx`: a leg midpoint is a fine place to hang a leg's CAPTION, but a transfer is a place, and averaging two stops would invent one. They render as `.mk--transfer` divs in the existing `.ov-layer` at `px(lat,lng)`, mirroring `legLabels` — hollow ring + change glyph so they never read as another stop on the day, `z-index: 3` so a stop marker wins an overlap. **THE FIXTURE SUPPLIES DATA, NOT A SECOND PARSER**: `mockLeg` now describes its ride as provider-shaped steps and reads them back through the REAL `extractTransitSegments` (exported for exactly this), and takes the leg's departure instant — threaded from the travel route and both engines' deps, accumulated in `mockTravelLegs` the way `getTravelLegs` does — so its board/alight times land inside the leg the schedule actually builds. Its minutes and mode are unchanged, so no e2e pin moved. It stays ONE ride on purpose (a fixture transfer would rewrite the leg line `fixtures.spec` pins), which means **transfer markers have no mock coverage at all** — the same live-verify-only wall real map geometry has. **TESTS: +4 travel cases** (instants survive per ride; coordinates survive on both sides of the transfer; a response with names only yields nulls rather than throwing or fabricating; junk times and half-coordinates are dropped while the rest of the ride survives), **a new 18-case `transitDetail.test.ts`** (the four instants, the per-ride pairs, all four fallbacks, three staleness shapes, and the transfer rules incl. "neither coordinate → no marker"), **a new 7-case `schemas.test.ts`** — the server validator had no direct test before — and **+2 `clientPayloads` cases** pinning that a leg WITH the fields passes, a leg WITHOUT them still does, and a corrupt time or coordinate is rejected. `fixtures.spec.ts` gained the end-to-end proof that the leg expands into four rows in a real browser. **REVERT-RUN, discriminating**: making the parser write nulls again turned exactly the two "survive onto the ride" cases red (19/21) while the null-response and junk-input cases stayed green; restored and re-verified 21/21. **GATE:** `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors with the same 11 long-standing warnings, typecheck clean, 46 unit suites, webpack build, 81/81 mock e2e (including both clock-dependent stated-window cases, run early). Also eyeballed on a mock production server on :3200 (port 3000 untouched): collapsed and expanded cards render as intended, the card widens without overflowing the strip, the walk leg is unchanged, and zero transfer markers appear on a single-ride plan.

## Feature: you can start the day from outside the city you're planning in
Goal: let someone who lives in a suburb plan a day in the city. The starting address had to BE in the selected city — a Mississauga address for a Toronto plan was refused outright — which is wrong about how people actually use this: you leave from where you live and go where the day is. The refusal still has to exist for an address that is genuinely wrong (a typo'd city, the wrong Springfield); what had to change is what "wrong" means.
What should be done: replace the locality-name equality that decided this with a DISTANCE from the city's centre, keep the country check hard, keep the region check as a secondary signal, stop the query builder from forcing the address into the city, fix the map so a far start doesn't crush the city stops into one dot, and move the two things that were quietly sampled at the start rather than the city — the weather and the plan's timezone — onto the city, since the day happens there.
What was done (technical): **THE RULE IS NOW A DISTANCE, AND CODE OWNS IT.** `judgeStartProximity` (pure, in `geocode.ts`) is the whole decision: haversine from the geocoded start to the city's LOCATION — not its viewport, whose size varies far too much between cities to compare against — with `MAX_START_DISTANCE_FROM_CITY_METERS` at 75 km. That covers every real commute in (Mississauga ~22 km, Oakville ~35 km, Hamilton ~58 km) and still refuses Barrie at ~90 km. Past it is a new `geocode_far_from_city` (422) whose message NAMES the city, because "very far from where?" is not an actionable refusal. The country check is untouched and still runs first; `geocode_incomplete_address` is untouched. **The region check survives as a SECONDARY signal, and "secondary" is load-bearing**: a mismatch refuses only beyond `SAME_METRO_METERS` (25 km), because inside that radius every cross-region address is a real metro suburb rather than a mistake — Gatineau QC for an Ottawa plan is 2 km away, Jersey City NJ for New York is 10 km. A hard region drop would have re-created the bug this change removes, one field over. **`buildGeocodeUrl` no longer appends the city's locality to the address text.** It had to go or the guardrail could never see the address it was meant to judge: "12 Elm St, Mississauga" + ", Toronto" is a self-contradictory query, and Google answers it by relocating the result into Toronto or flagging `partial_match`. Region and country stay in the suffix, the viewport still biases ranking. **A LIVE RUN CAUGHT WHAT THE UNIT TESTS COULD NOT.** Toronto's `administrativeArea` was resolving to "Toronto", because `parseResult` asked for `administrative_area_level_1` and `level_2` in one call and took whichever the provider listed first — and for Toronto level_2 is also spelled "Toronto". So the city's "region" was the city's own name, in the query suffix AND in the region check: the locality rule was still running, wearing a region's clothes. Proof from :3200 against the real Geocoding API — an Oakville start refused as a different region, and a Hamilton address relocated INTO Toronto. Level_1 now wins outright, level_2 survives only as the fallback for countries that have no level_1. After the fix the live matrix reads: Toronto/Mississauga/Oakville/Hamilton resolved, Barrie and Ottawa `geocode_far_from_city`. **≥2 survivors still go to the existing `recovery.mode:"geocode"` panel** — and dropping the locality rule strengthens it, because an out-of-city address and its in-city lookalike now BOTH survive to be chosen between instead of being silently reduced to the in-city one. **THE MAP FIT (`ItineraryMap.tsx`) — the approach chosen, and why the first one was wrong.** Home joins the opening fit only while it doesn't take the frame over, and the test is RELATIVE (`max(stopsRadius, 1_500) * 3`) because "too far" is a fact about the plan's own size, not a number of kilometres. It was written first as a flat 40 km ceiling; the live Oakville plan at 35 km passed that ceiling and put both downtown stops on top of each other, which is the exact failure the cap exists to prevent — so the ceiling became a ratio. Past it the view fits the STOPS; the home marker is still drawn at its real coordinates and its route still leads off-frame toward it. Nothing about where home sits is faked to keep it on screen, and the in-city case (every mock e2e, every plan before this) fits exactly as it did. **WEATHER AND TIMEZONE NOW COME FROM THE CITY.** Both were read off the start address, which was the same point back when a start had to be in the city and is not any more. The forecast gates OUTDOOR VENUES, and those venues are in the city, so a suburb's rain was answering the wrong question; the zone drives every scheduled time and every label in the plan, and 75 km is enough to cross a zone boundary. The zone is simply the city candidate's (`planZone` no longer gets reassigned from the address), and the city's centre rides on the parse as `parsed.cityCenter` — injected by the app beside `parsed.city`/`parsed.home`, never LLM-inferred — so `swap.ts`'s `weatherFor` and `reroute.ts`'s `weatherOrigin` sample the city too. Both fall back to home when it is absent, which is every plan made before this. **`selectVenues`' distance line was re-read and reworded** (quality, not blocking): it told the model to "NEVER pick a venue tens of kilometres away", which from a suburb is true of every candidate that exists — always-true noise that can leak into a pick reason, and worse, a relative preference that would drag the whole outing to whichever edge of the city is nearest home. It now compares candidates with EACH OTHER rather than an absolute number, says outright that the start may sit outside the area being planned, and forbids relocating the outing toward it. No code change: `kmFromHome` is still a real fact and is still computed by code. **The distance cap also closes a hole nobody had filled**: there was no cap on a start ANYWHERE, so a 200 km typo geocoded fine and became a silent multi-hour home→first-stop leg (`getSingleLeg` estimates any distance). Confirmed rather than assumed — a refused address throws a 422 out of `resolvePlace`, so no itinerary is created and no leg is ever computed. **TESTS: this rule had none.** `geocode.test.ts` goes 9 → 16 cases, with a helper that runs the rule's own haversine backwards so the 75 km boundary is pinned exactly on both sides rather than near it: a suburb inside the cap resolves, the cap itself resolves, past it is `geocode_far_from_city`, a cross-region address refuses beyond the metro radius and resolves inside it, an out-of-city address and its in-city twin both reach the candidate panel, and `judgeStartProximity` is exercised directly at all four boundaries. Wrong-country and incomplete-address cases still pass unchanged. Both revert-runs were done and restored: disabling the distance comparison turned "beyond the cap is refused" and the pure-helper case red (14/16), and re-adding the locality suffix turned the URL case red (15/16) — that suffix's effect happens inside Google, so the URL assertion is the only place it can be pinned, which is why the suburb case (a `resolveGeocodeResponse` test) does not see it. Mock e2e still cannot reach any of this: `mockGeocodingResponse` returns fixed Chestnut coordinates for city AND address, so a mock start is always 0 m from its city — the same wall the other geocode-validation cases have always had, and no fixture was bent to fake a distance. Gate: lint 0 errors / 11 baseline warnings, typecheck clean, 44 unit suites, build, 81/81 e2e.

## Feature: the account corner becomes one name and a dropdown
Goal: stop the corner from growing a pill per screen. It had three side by side — History, your name, Sign out — which was already one too many, and every future account screen would have added another; a menu is the shape that stops counting.
What should be done: make the name the single trigger, put Preferences / History / Sign out inside it, set Sign out apart because it ends the session rather than opening a panel, build the menu minimally in the app's own style with no new library, keep the panels' existing mounts and gating so only their TRIGGERS move, and make sure a menu belonging to whoever just signed out cannot linger for whoever signs in next.
What was done (technical): **ONE TRIGGER, THREE DESTINATIONS, IN A NEW `AccountMenu.tsx`.** The `.acct__who` pill keeps every rule it had — avatar or monogram, name clamp, hover lift — and swaps `aria-haspopup="dialog"` for `aria-haspopup="menu"` plus an `aria-expanded` that tracks the state, gaining only a caret. The two standalone pills beside it are gone: `.acct__out` was deleted outright (CSS included, and from the mobile 44px list and the reduced-motion list that both still named it), and History moved inside. **`role="menu"` IS A PROMISE ABOUT KEYBOARD BEHAVIOUR, SO THE BEHAVIOUR IS THERE**: focus lands on the first item when it opens, arrows move and wrap between items, Home/End jump, Escape closes and hands focus back to the trigger, Tab leaves, ArrowDown on the closed trigger opens it, and an outside POINTERDOWN closes without stealing focus — pointerdown rather than click because a menu waiting for mouseup stays open under a finger through a scroll, and the trigger is excluded from that listener or the same press would close and reopen in one go. What is deliberately absent is a focus trap: a menu is not a dialog, and trapping focus in one is how a keyboard user gets stuck in a corner of the hero. The items are read out of the DOM by `[role="menuitem"]` rather than through a parallel ref array — three buttons in one place do not need a second list to keep in step. **SIGN OUT IS SET APART BY A RULE AND A TREATMENT, and that is the one visual rule this file really makes**: Preferences and History open something you can close again, Sign out ends the session, and a flat list of three would put the destructive one at the same weight and the same distance from the pointer as its neighbours. Verified in a real browser: a 1px separator above it, and a distinctly softer ink (`rgba(30,58,76,0.78)` against the items' `rgb(30,58,76)`). **THE GUEST KEEPS THE HISTORY PILL, AND THAT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT OF THE BRIEF.** History is offered to EVERYONE by an existing, documented decision — a guest who opens it meets the reason to sign in, and that empty state IS the nudge. There is no account menu on the guest branch to move it into, so removing the pill outright would have made the panel unreachable by exactly the people its guest state is written for; the pill therefore stays, in the guest branch only, and the signed-in corner is the single control the brief asked for. **THE OPEN STATE LIVES IN `page.tsx`, NOT IN THE COMPONENT**, because the survey's identity re-arm has to be able to shut it: the corner unmounts during the guest hop every account switch passes through, so an open menu is merely INVISIBLE rather than closed, and without `setAccountMenuOpen(false)` beside the existing `setProfileOpen(false)` it would reappear over the next account's name. Choosing any item closes first and then acts, so a panel never opens over a menu still reporting `aria-expanded="true"`. **CSS IS A NEW `.acctmenu` BLOCK ON THE CORNER'S OWN PALETTE** — translucent-white pill language, card surface, `--rule` separator, teal-tinted hover — and it is an ANCHORED popover rather than an overlay, which is why it needs no `.empty > *:not(…)` exclusion entry: that rule only selects direct children of `.empty`, and this is a grandchild of the already-excluded `.acct`, whose `z-index: 3` stacking context the popover's `z-index: 4` lives inside. Confirmed in the browser against the real stylesheet with the component's own markup: wrapper `position: relative`, popover `position: absolute / z-index: 4 / visible`, laid out below the trigger and inside the viewport, three 38px items (44px under the 560px media query), and the reserved acid green resolving on nothing. **NO MOCK E2E, for the documented reason**: the trigger renders only inside `signedInForReal && auth.user`, mock runs have no Firebase, so the corner is permanently the guest branch there — the same wall signed-in history and the profile panel already record. What WAS proved without an account is the guest contract itself, in a real browser on a production build: the corner reads exactly "History Sign in", with no `.acct__who`, no `.acctmenu` and no `.prof` anywhere in the DOM.

## Feature: "Other" in the foods question becomes a cuisine you can actually type
Goal: let someone whose cuisine is not one of the curated eight say so, and have it MEAN something — reach the planner as a real preference, not a chip that gets dropped on read. Until now "Other" named nothing, so it was the one option in the whole survey whose answer nothing could act on.
What should be done: store the typed text as a sibling field rather than smuggling it into the slug arrays, because the closed-set guarantee is the property worth protecting; sanitize it hard, since free text bypasses every invariant that holds for a phrase a human wrote into a table; project it into the planner's foods list so it becomes "<cuisine> restaurant" exactly like the curated ones; and — the part that is net-new — stop two PREFERENCES from manufacturing a contradiction refusal between them.
What was done (technical): **A SIBLING FIELD, NOT A FIFTH FOOD.** `TasteProfileDocument` gains `foodsOther: string`, flat beside the arrays like every other stored field, always a string and `""` when unset (Firestore rejects undefined, the same reason empty dimensions are `[]`); `TasteProfilePayload` follows through the existing `Omit`. A document written before the field simply has no key and reads as `""` — no migration, no backfill, exactly how `activities` shipped. The EDITABLE shape needed a companion because `TasteAnswers` is `Record<TasteDimension, string[]>` and cannot hold a string, so it became an interface extending that record with an optional nested `other?: Partial<Record<TasteDimension, string>>`: `answers[q.id]` still means what it meant, every consumer that maps `SURVEY_QUESTIONS` is untouched, and the key is OMITTED when empty so `EMPTY_ANSWERS` is still literally the empty answer set and four existing deep-equality tests kept passing unchanged. `answersFromProfile` is the one function that knows both shapes. **THE CAP IS 40 CHARACTERS, AND THE SANITIZER IS THE POINT.** `normalizeFreeText` strips everything outside `[A-Za-z -]` to a SPACE (so "thai/korean" is two words, never one invented one), collapses hyphen and whitespace runs, trims separators off both ends because the invariant demands a LETTER first, caps at `MAX_FREE_TEXT_LENGTH`, then trims again — the cut lands wherever it lands and must not leave a dangling separator. Empty after sanitizing is UNSET, so "!!!", "12345" and a lone emoji are the same as never having typed. 40 is generous for "Trinidadian roti" and still a ceiling, and the ceiling is the reason it exists: this string is read back server-side on EVERY plan and handed to the model, and `buildQuery` can splice a projected phrase into a Places query verbatim — which is why the curated phrases are pinned against `/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z -]*$/` and why a test now sweeps ten hostile inputs through the sanitizer and asserts every survivor clears the same regex. It runs on WRITE (via `normalizeTasteAnswers`, which `POST /api/profile` already calls), on READ out of storage, and again at projection — a record is only ever as trustworthy as the client that wrote it. **THE "OTHER" GATE LIVES IN EXACTLY ONE FUNCTION.** Text only counts while the chip is pressed: un-ticking hides the box, and a preference nobody can see steering their plans is the worst version of this feature. `gatedFreeText` is called by both `normalizeTasteAnswers` and `freeTextAnswer`, so storage and the screen cannot disagree, and the slug arrays are never touched — a test asserts the free text can appear in none of the four dimensions and that "other" survives `normalizeAnswerSet` as the ordinary option it always was. **THE PROJECTION IS ONE MORE FOOD PHRASE, APPENDED.** `toPlannerPreferences` pushes the sanitized text onto `foods` after the curated ones (a typed answer breaks ties toward the deliberate one), which makes the free-text-alone case non-null for free; the `other` SLUG stays in `NON_PLANNING_OPTIONS`, because it still names no cuisine and injecting the literal word "other" would put it in someone's dinner search. **THE CONTRADICTION GUARD IS THE LOAD-BEARING ADDITION, AND IT REUSES THE EXISTING MACHINERY RATHER THAN INVENTING A SHAPE.** A stored "vegetarian" plus a typed "steakhouse" are two preferences, NEITHER of them restated by the request: let both through and the model writes both into searchQueries, `category_signals` ends up holding both halves of a `DIETARY_VENUE_CONFLICTS` pair, `contradictionReason` fires, and `page.tsx` turns it into a hard refusal the user did nothing to earn. `dietaryConflictsWithText` runs the typed food against each diet that SURVIVED the existing prompt filter — a diet already dropped cannot fight anything — and also against the RAW PROMPT, because "a vegan dinner" plus a typed "seafood" is the identical failure with the diet stated instead of stored; in both directions it calls `dietaryConflictsWithPrompt`, so whatever pair the guard would refuse on is by construction a pair we decline to inject, and the table is never re-encoded. Only the free text is dropped: the diet, the curated foods, the style and the activities all still apply. Recorded rather than papered over: it does not strip the accommodation phrasing `contradictionReason` does, so "somewhere with vegan options" plus a typed "seafood" over-suppresses for that one plan — losing a cuisine hint is the cheap direction, a refusal is not. **THE LEAK STRIP WAS DELIBERATELY NOT EXTENDED**, and ACTIVITIES free text was deliberately not built: `isLeakedActivityConstraint` is an EQUALITY test that is only safe because every injected activity phrase is a place KIND that can never be a provable constraint, and a user-typed activity is free to be "live music", which is real and provable — so a typed activity would either leak or start deleting things people actually asked for. Foods carries no new exposure a curated "Japanese" did not already carry. `FREE_TEXT_DIMENSION` names the one dimension with a box and documents why dietary's "Other" does not get one. **UI IS RENDERING-ONLY IN THE SHARED GRID.** `TasteQuestions` gained a required `onOtherChange` (optional would let a screen render the box and silently drop what is typed) and renders a `<label>`-wrapped input — wrapped rather than wired by id, because two screens can hold this grid and duplicate ids would break the association — with `maxLength` matching the storage cap so the limit happens where the user can see it. Both screens hold the state themselves: the survey counts a typed cuisine toward its "Save preferences" label, and the panel routes the box and the chips through one new `beginEdit` so both clear a stale "Saved" and answer a standing discard prompt identically. Text is held RAW while typing — sanitizing per keystroke eats the space in "soul food" the moment it is pressed. **DIRTY-TRACKING COMPARES IT SANITIZED, which is the honest definition**: the flag answers "would saving change what is stored", so "Ethiopian!" over a stored "Ethiopian" is clean and must not light up Save or prompt on close, while "Thai" over "Ethiopian" is dirty. Without extending `answersEqual` at all the box would accept text and Save would stay disabled forever — the feature would look broken in the editor. The panel's save is unchanged in every other respect: the text rides INSIDE the answers object, so Trap 1 (the no-merge wipe) stays handled by the same "send all four, always" rule, and Trap 2 stays handled because the panel still has no writer of its own. **TESTS: 46 → 60 `tastePreferences`, 25 → 35 `plannerPreferences`, 13 → 17 `profileEdit`** — the sanitizer exhaustively (trim, collapse, strip, control characters and a zero-width space, the leading/trailing separator, the cap and the cut that lands on a space, empty-is-unset, non-strings, and the query-safety sweep), the gate, the closed-set guarantee, the storage round trip with `""` when unset and a pre-field document, and on the projection side the append, the slug still being dropped, free-text-alone staying non-null, junk still returning null, the read-side sanitize, and the suppression pinned four ways including a sweep over every conflicting pair against three prompts asserting the surviving projection never manufactures a refusal. **TWO REVERT-RUNS, BOTH DISCRIMINATING.** (A) Suppression disabled → exactly 4 red, all of them the suppression cases, with the "unrelated request keeps the typed food" case and every other projection case staying green. (B) The punctuation strip removed from `normalizeFreeText` → 7 red in `tastePreferences` (the strip, control characters, empty-is-unset, query-safety, the normaliser, and both storage cases that depend on it) plus 2 in `plannerPreferences` (the read-side sanitize and the junk-stays-null case), while the trim, collapse and cap cases stayed green because they do not depend on the strip. Both restored and re-verified. **NO MOCK E2E IS POSSIBLE**, same wall as the rest of this branch: the box lives inside the survey and the profile panel, and mock runs have no Firebase, so neither screen can be reached. The guest contract WAS verified live on a production build — `GET /api/profile` → `{profile:null,readFailed:false}`, and an unauthenticated POST carrying `other:{foods:"<script>steakhouse!!</script>"}` → `{saved:false}`, nothing stored, no error. **GATE:** `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors with the same 11 long-standing warnings, typecheck clean, 44 unit suites, webpack production build, 81/81 mock e2e including both clock-dependent stated-window cases.

## Feature: you can change your taste answers — the onboarding survey gets a re-openable editor
Goal: click your name in the account corner and get your four stored taste answers back, editable, with a Save that means something — because until now the survey was a one-shot capture with no way back into it, and the answers it files now decide which KIND of stop a vague day gets. Being stuck with a preference you gave once, in thirty seconds, on your first login, is the wrong kind of permanent.
What should be done: assemble it out of pieces that already exist and already work — `SURVEY_QUESTIONS`, `GET`/`POST /api/profile`, `normalizeTasteAnswers`, HistoryPanel's overlay shape — rather than growing an endpoint, a store or a schema for it; guard the two traps that make this feature dangerous rather than merely fiddly (a write with no merge, and a planner that reads the profile back server-side); do NOT reuse `TasteSurvey`, whose five onboarding behaviours are each wrong here; add the app's first confirm-on-discard and keep it small; and leave the onboarding path byte-identical.
What was done (technical): **THE PANEL IS A SIBLING OF THE SURVEY AND SHARES EXACTLY ONE THING WITH IT.** `TasteSurvey` starts blank, is shown once, writes a skip on escape, and closes itself two seconds after submitting behind a "you're all set" send-off. Every one of those is wrong for a screen you can re-open — a blank start would erase what is stored, an escape that writes would file an accidental answer, and a form you can leave without saving has to say so. What the two genuinely share is the question GRID, so that and nothing else was extracted: `TasteQuestions.tsx`, a presentational component that maps `SURVEY_QUESTIONS` into the fieldset + `aria-pressed` chips, on the survey's own `taste__*` classes so restyling a chip restyles both screens and the two cannot drift. `TasteSurvey`'s own diff is that one substitution; its state, toggle, footer, escape-skip and timer are untouched. **TRAP 1 IS A WIPE, AND ITS DANGEROUS HALF IS NOT THE OBVIOUS HALF.** `writeTasteProfile` does `set` with NO merge and `normalizeTasteAnswers` fills every missing dimension with `[]`, so a save that omitted one would silently erase it — handled by sending all four, always, seeded from what was read. The half that is easy to miss is the FAILED READ: an editor that opened on a broken read would be pre-filled with blanks and would offer a Save button that erases real answers. So `parseProfileEditLoad` reports `readFailed` and the panel renders an error with NO editor and NO Save at all. That is the same trade `shouldShowSurvey` already makes on the same document — on a failed read, do the thing that cannot destroy anything — and it reuses `profileGateState` to decide what "failed" means, rather than adding a second definition on the editor's side. **TRAP 2 IS THE RACE THAT WAS CLOSED ON 2026-08-09, AND A NEW FETCH WOULD HAVE RE-OPENED IT.** `/api/parse` reads this profile back SERVER-SIDE on every plan, and `planFrom` waits on `settlePendingWrite(pendingProfileWrite)`; a POST issued from inside the panel would never land in that ref, so edit → close → plan immediately would have returned a plan built from the OLD profile. The panel therefore has no writer of its own. `saveTasteProfile` in `page.tsx` now RETURNS its promise — `Promise<ProfileSaveResult>` — while still storing the `void` form in `pendingProfileWrite`, and the two callers take opposite halves of that: the survey `void`s it and keeps its send-off, the editor awaits it, because an explicit Save button with no acknowledgement reads as broken. ONE writer, two dispositions; the catch stays inside, so the promise always resolves and the plan-side wait inherits the guarantee it had before. **THE SAVE-RESULT PATH IS ADDITIVE**: `parseSaveResult` is strict (`saved === true`, nothing else counts), because the screen says "Saved" on the strength of it, and `{saved:false}` — the honest answer when there is nowhere to write — becomes "Couldn't save", not an exception. **DIRTY-TRACKING IS PURE, AND ORDER IS NOT DIRT.** `answersDirty`/`answersEqual` (new `app/lib/profileEdit.ts`) compare per dimension as SETS and iterate `SURVEY_QUESTIONS`, so a fifth dimension is covered by adding it to the survey. Clean closes at once — interrupting someone who changed nothing is a dialog that only ever gets in the way — and dirty shows "Discard changes?" in the panel's own footer, replacing the actions, with "Keep editing" focused and Escape stepping back to it: the safe default at every level is the one that destroys nothing. A successful save makes what was saved the new clean baseline, so closing straight after saving does not prompt. The panel also re-emits every toggle through `normalizeAnswerSet`, so the on-screen set stays in the question's canonical order and is byte-comparable with the stored one. **REACHABILITY IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN CHECKED.** The `.acct__who` pill became a `<button aria-haspopup="dialog">` keeping its avatar, clamp and pill styling (plus the hover lift the other account controls have, since it is now interactive), and it renders ONLY inside the `signedInForReal && auth.user` branch — a guest sees `.acct__signin`, so there is nothing to press. The mount is gated on `signedInForReal` too, so signing out with it open takes it off screen, and one line was added beside the survey's re-arm to close it on an identity change: a panel still open belongs to the person who is no longer here. Landing-only, like History. `.prof` was added to the `.empty > *:not(…)` exclusion list — the documented defect where an overlay is silently demoted to `position: relative` — and that was VERIFIED in a real browser rather than assumed (`position: fixed`, `z-index: 60`, inset 0). **`completed: true` ON AN EDIT, AND THE NAG STAYS DEAD:** `surveyCompleted` gates nothing (`plannerPreferences` never consults it) so true is the honest value for an answer deliberately given, `surveySeen` is hardcoded true by the one writer, `shouldRearmSurvey` is uid-keyed and an edit changes no uid, and `profileGate` is "present" for anyone who can open the panel — three independent reasons the onboarding survey cannot come back. **TESTS ARE THE PURE SEAMS, 13 cases in a new `profileEdit.test.ts`** (43 → 44 unit suites): the seed projection including a pre-activities document and a retired slug, the failed-read guard, dirty across all four dimensions, order-independence, subset-vs-superset, and the save-result strictness. **THREE REVERT-RUNS, AND THEY DISCRIMINATE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS**, because a dirty flag fails two ways and the suite has to tell them apart. (A) `answersDirty` forced to always-false → exactly 3 red, all of them the is-a-change cases, and every clean-side case stayed green. (B) Forced to always-true → 4 red, a DISJOINT set apart from the composition case, all of them clean-side. (C) The failed-read guard short-circuited → exactly 1 red, the wipe-trap case. All restored and re-verified 13/13. **NO MOCK E2E WAS ADDED, DELIBERATELY:** the panel needs a real non-anonymous account and mock runs have no Firebase at all, so `signedInForReal` is never true and the trigger never renders — the same wall signed-in history already documents. A spec that cannot run is worse than none. **GATE:** `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors with the same 11 long-standing warnings, typecheck clean, 44 unit suites, webpack production build, **81/81 mock e2e** including both clock-dependent stated-window cases (the run was at 15:01 local, well before the hour that clamps them). **LIVE-VERIFIED on a webpack production build at :3200, port 3000 untouched:** the guest contract holds (`GET /api/profile` → `{profile:null,readFailed:false}`, unauthenticated `POST` → `{saved:false}`), a real browser on the landing page shows `.acct__signin` with NO `.acct__who` trigger and no `.prof` in the DOM, the overlay computes to `position: fixed / z-index: 60` under `.empty`, the footer's 44px targets and the quiet disabled Save render as intended, and no element anywhere in the panel resolves to the reserved acid green. **NOT VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, STATED PLAINLY:** the panel driven by React needs a real Google popup an agent must not complete, so the pre-fill, the save round trip, the discard prompt and the post-edit plan are the owner's to eyeball — the same boundary 3A, 3B and the activities entry all recorded. What stands behind the visual half is the CSS exercised against the real stylesheet with the component's own markup.

## Fix: the taste survey was latched to the PAGE SESSION, so the second account of a session was never asked
Goal: sign in, get the survey, sign out, sign in as somebody else in the same tab — and that second person gets asked too, with no refresh. Today they are not, and a refresh fixes it, which is the same shape as the previous entry's bug and the same reason it hid: the person testing has already been onboarded, and the app looks right to everyone except the one user who is new.
What should be done: reset the one-shot latch when the signed-in USER changes, keyed on the uid rather than on auth events — `onIdTokenChanged` now fires on every hourly token refresh, and a latch reset on each of those would re-pop the survey mid-session for the person who just answered it. Prove the itinerary is untouched: ownership, storage and the resume-on-refresh effect must behave BYTE-IDENTICALLY when accounts switch, because the survey and the plan share no state and this fix must not give them any. Extract the decision as a pure function and test it exhaustively; the survey must only be RE-ARMED, never forced open.
What was done (technical): **THE LATCH WAS ONLY HALF THE STALENESS.** `surveyOffered` (a ref, set true when the survey mounts) suppressed account 2 through `shouldShowSurvey`'s first line, but `profileGate` was stale in exactly the same way and would have suppressed it independently: it still held account 1's `"present"`, and the `/api/profile` effect is guarded by `if (profileGate !== "unknown") return`, so the new user's profile was never read. Resetting only the latch would have moved the failure one line down and looked like a partial fix. **The pure decision is `shouldRearmSurvey(previousUid, currentUid)`** in `tastePreferences.ts` beside `shouldShowSurvey`: true only for a change between two real uids, false for a token refresh (same uid), false while there is no current uid, false for the first identity of a session, and blank-is-absent on both sides. `page.tsx` holds the uid it describes in `surveyIdentityUid`, a ref that is only ever written with a REAL uid — the null the observer delivers between `signOut()` and the anonymous sign-in that replaces it is an absence, not a person, and recording it would compare the next identity against null and never re-arm at all. **The re-arm lives INSIDE the existing gate effect and RETURNS without deciding**, which is the part that is not obvious: as a separate effect it would race the gate within the same commit, where `profileGate` still reads stale, and a non-anonymous → non-anonymous switch would show account 2 a survey computed from account 1's profile. Returning early means the decision is only ever made on a gate the current user's own read filled in; `setProfileGate("unknown")` is what re-fires that read (it is in the read effect's dependency array) and what cancels one still in flight. An open survey is closed on the switch, because submitting it would file the previous person's answers under the new account's token. A `[survey-gate]` log records WHETHER an identity change re-armed, never WHICH — the same line `planner_plan`'s `personalized` flag draws. **THE ITINERARY IS UNTOUCHED, AND SHARES NO STATE BY CONSTRUCTION:** `ownership.ts` is pure and server-side, keyed off the verified token; the store is not involved; the resume effect is one-shot on `resumeAttempted` and keyed on `auth.status` + `authHeaders`, neither of which this fix writes, and it does not read `auth.user` at all — so a uid change cannot re-trigger, clear or re-own a plan, and an in-progress itinerary crosses an account switch exactly as it does today. 8 new cases in `tastePreferences.test.ts` (46 total, up from 38), including the composition that IS the bug: latched + absent = not asked, re-armed + absent = asked, re-armed + present = still not asked. Revert-run: forcing `shouldRearmSurvey` to always return false failed exactly the three account-switch cases and nothing else; restored. Full gate green (0 lint errors / 11 long-standing warnings, tsc clean, 43 unit suites, build, 81/81 e2e at 04:45 local — both clock-dependent window tests passed). Live on :3200: guest path unchanged (one `/api/itinerary` resume, zero `/api/profile`, clean console, no effect loop), a real plan resumed byte-identically across a refresh. THE ACCOUNT-SWITCH SYMPTOM ITSELF NEEDS TWO REAL GOOGLE ACCOUNTS AND IS THE OWNER'S TO EYEBALL — mock e2e has no Firebase at all, so `auth.user` is null there and this code path is inert (which is also why all 81 are unaffected).

## Fix: two first-run timing bugs that made the taste survey look broken to the only people who see it
Goal: a brand-new user signs in with Google and gets the survey THERE AND THEN, in that session, with no refresh — and the first plan they make after submitting it reflects the answers they just gave, also with no refresh. Both were true only after a reload, which is the worst possible shape for an onboarding bug: it works perfectly for everyone who has already been onboarded, and never for the one person it exists to serve.
What should be done: fix the two INDEPENDENTLY, because they share a theme ("act after the profile settles") and nothing else — one is a client read that never fires, the other is a write a read overtakes; ship them together, because fixing the first alone makes the second bite MORE often; change no gate, no route, no store and no trust boundary; and put a guard on the first fix before it can introduce a churn problem of its own.
What was done (technical): **BUG 1 WAS NOT A GATE PROBLEM, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED A READ OF THE GATE.** `shouldShowSurvey` is correct and its call site already re-evaluates — `profileGate` is in the effect's dependency array, so the read landing DOES re-run it. The break was one level upstream and one layer down, in the SDK: `signIn()` upgrades a guest with `linkWithPopup` because that KEEPS THE UID (a deliberate, correct decision — a plain `signInWithPopup` would mint a new uid and orphan the plan the user is in the middle of), and `onAuthStateChanged` fires ONLY on a uid change. `@firebase/auth`'s `notifyAuthListeners` is explicit: `idTokenSubscription.next(...)` unconditionally, then `if (this.lastNotifiedUid !== currentUid)` before the auth-state one. Same uid, so the listener stayed silent while `_reloadWithoutSaving` flipped `isAnonymous` to false ON THE SAME OBJECT — invisible to React, which held a snapshot from `toAppUser`. `signedInForReal` stayed false, the `/api/profile` read guarded by it never fired, `profileGate` never left "unknown", and the survey never mounted. Also silent for the same reason, and fixed by the same line: the login card not dismissing itself, the account corner still offering "Sign in", and `HistoryPanel` still being told `isGuest`. Only the brand-new-account path was ever affected — a RETURNING Google account hits the link conflict and falls to `signInWithCredential`, which mints a different uid and fires normally, which is exactly why this reproduced for new users only. **THE FIX IS ONE IMPORT: `onIdTokenChanged` INSTEAD OF `onAuthStateChanged`**, the strict superset Firebase's own type docs point at ("To keep the old behavior, see {@link onIdTokenChanged}" on one; "includes sign-in, sign-out, and token refresh events" on the other). Nothing else about the subscription moved — same `toAppUser` mapping, same statuses, same error path, same unsubscribe — and the popup's return value is still never read, because the observer being the single source of truth is the property that keeps the login card and the account corner from disagreeing. **THE MANDATORY GUARD, WHICH IS THE REAL DESIGN WORK.** The superset fires on every token refresh — hourly, and again on each `getIdToken()` that renews, which this app does on EVERY authenticated request — and `toAppUser` mints a fresh object each time, so storing them all would churn `user`'s IDENTITY on a timer and re-run every effect keyed on it. That is how a fix for a missing update becomes a source of spurious ones. So the callback stores a new user only when `sameAppUserIdentity` says an observable field moved, via `setUser(current => current && same(current, mapped) ? current : mapped)` — the functional form, so the effect keeps its `[]` deps and React bails out on the returned-identical value. `setStatus` stays unconditional (an identical string is not a re-render). **THE COMPARISON IS EVERY FIELD THE UI READS, NOT A HAND-PICKED SUBSET** — uid, isAnonymous, displayName, email AND photoURL. One deviation from the task, stated: photoURL was not on its list of four. It is rendered by the account corner, so leaving it out makes a changed avatar a field that can silently go stale, and none of the five move on a token refresh, so including it costs the guard exactly nothing. **ALL THREE `auth.user` CONSUMERS WERE REASONED THROUGH WITH THE GUARD IN PLACE** rather than assumed: the survey gate effect is idempotent behind `surveyOffered` and modifies none of its own deps; the profile read re-runs on the `profileGate` it sets but returns immediately unless it is "unknown", which is the pre-existing design; the resume effect never depended on `auth.user` at all (`auth.status` plus a stable `authHeaders`, itself a `useCallback` on a `[]`-dep `getIdToken`). None can loop, and with the guard the first two now see FEWER identity changes than before, not more. **BUG 2 IS AN ORDERING BUG AND WAS FIXED BY ORDERING, NOTHING ELSE.** `saveTasteProfile` was `void (async () => …)()` with no handle kept, the confirmation closes on a 2s timer with zero coupling to it, and `/api/parse` reads the profile back SERVER-SIDE for the verified caller — so a user who submitted and immediately planned could beat their own write, on the coldest path a session has (a first `verifyIdToken` fetches Google's signing certs, a first Firestore call opens its channel, both BEFORE the document is touched). The write promise is now kept in a `pendingProfileWrite` ref and `planFrom` opens with `settlePendingWrite(...)`. **IN `planFrom`, NOT `runPipeline`, DELIBERATELY:** all three planner entry points — first plan, answered clarifying questions, geocode-ambiguity resume — funnel through it, and it sits after the geocode, so the round trip the plan was making anyway is time the write gets for free. The request body is byte-identical and no preference rides on it: identity still comes from the bearer token alone. **THE CATCH STAYS INSIDE THE WRITER SO THE PROMISE ALWAYS RESOLVES** — whoever waits is waiting for the write to be OVER, not to have worked, because a plan is not the place to report that a preference did not save. `settlePendingWrite` catches again anyway (belt to that braces, so the guarantee belongs to the helper rather than to a well-behaved caller), adds no second timeout (the bound is `fetchJson`'s existing 15s), returns immediately on a null ref — every plan but the one after a submit — and clears the ref only if it still holds the promise it awaited, so a newer write started mid-wait is not dropped back into the race. **NOTHING WAS DONE INSIDE `TasteSurvey`:** the two-second send-off is unchanged, no spinner was added, and the screen still does not know the write exists. **TESTS ARE THE PURE SEAMS, EXTRACTED FOR THAT PURPOSE** — `useAuth` cannot be unit-tested without a live SDK, so the two decisions worth pinning were lifted out of it: `sameAppUserIdentity` into `authUser.ts` (existing suite, 8→13 cases) and `settlePendingWrite` into a new `lib/pendingWrite.ts` (5 cases, all proving a SEQUENCE rather than a return value, since ordering is the entire subject). 42→43 unit suites. **THREE REVERT-RUNS, AND THEY DISCRIMINATE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS** because a diff-guard fails two ways and the suite has to tell them apart. (A) Guard forced to "always different" (the churn) → exactly the 2 no-change assertions red, all 3 is-a-change cases green. (B) Guard forced to "always same" (the original bug, the anonymity flip missed) → 4 red, and the token-refresh case stayed GREEN, correctly. (C) The plan gate made not to wait → exactly the ordering case red, with `["plan"]` recorded before the write. All restored and re-verified 13/13 and 5/5. **GATE:** `npm run check` green — lint 0 errors with the same 11 long-standing warnings, typecheck clean, 43 unit suites, webpack production build, and **81/81 mock e2e**, including the two clock-dependent stated-window cases, which pass because this run was at 02:25 local rather than the 20:34 that clamps them — the complement of the earlier proof that the clock and not the code decides those two. **LIVE-VERIFIED on a webpack production build at :3200, port 3000 untouched:** the guest contract holds (`GET /api/profile` → `{profile:null,readFailed:false}`); an unauthenticated `POST /api/parse` on "something to do tonight" still returns the un-personalized baseline (`live music venue` + `bar`, the open slot `confident:false` with the "what kind of thing" question, no constraints, `aesthetic: unspecified`); and in a real browser the whole guest path runs through the NEW subscription — one anonymous user persisted with `isAnonymous:true` and no providers, the page interactive, and `GET /api/itinerary` fired exactly once WITH a token, which happens only when the observer drives `auth.status` to "signed-in" and `getIdToken()` returns one. **NOT VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, STATED PLAINLY:** the anonymity flip itself and the personalized plan behind it need a real Google popup an agent must not complete — the same boundary 3A and the activities entry both recorded. What stands behind that half is the SDK source read directly (`notifyAuthListeners`, `_updateTokensIfNecessary`, `_link`, and the `linkWithPopup` → `LINK_VIA_POPUP` chain), the installed package's own doc comments, and the unit guard in both directions.

## Feature: activity-interest personalization — the profile now picks WHAT KIND of stop, not just its flavour
Goal: a signed-in user who says they like art, the outdoors, games, being active or live music should get vague-prompt plans that lean toward those KINDS of stops — "something to do tonight" landing on a gallery for an art lover and a live music venue for a music fan — while any explicit request overrides completely, exactly as cuisine already does.
What should be done: give the stored profile a path to activity-TYPE selection FIRST and prove it against a hardcoded profile with a real model, then and only then add the survey question; add `activities` as a fourth dimension end to end (projection, prompt, storage, survey); guard the one failure mode that would be catastrophic rather than merely wrong — an activity phrase reaching `constraints` — in CODE and not only in prompt wording; retire "cultural" from the style question so the same choice is not steered twice; and leave the no-profile path — every guest, every mock e2e run, everyone who skipped — planning exactly as it did before.
What was done (technical): **THE FINDING THAT SHAPED THE WHOLE CHANGE IS THAT `aesthetic` IS AN ADJECTIVE AND `searchQuery` IS A NOUN.** The three existing dimensions all shade WHAT A STOP IS LIKE: `style` becomes `context.aesthetic`, which `buildQuery` prepends verbatim to every category's Places text search, and `foods`/`dietary` colour a food stop's query. NONE of them decides WHICH STOPS EXIST — that is `activities[].searchQuery`, free text the planner writes and code never inspects, which becomes `category_signals` and then one Places search per entry. So "add a question" alone would have collected an answer with no path to the field that matters; the plumbing had to come first, and it did. **PART 1, VERIFIED BEFORE PART 2 EXISTED.** `activities` was added to the projection (`plannerPreferences.ts`), the planner prompt was told what to do with it, and the whole thing was run against HARDCODED profiles and the real `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` before a single survey option was written — nine scenarios, paced to stay under the 12k TPM ceiling. Results: `["art"]` → slot 0 `art gallery`; `["music"]` → `live music venue`; `["outdoors"]` → `park`; `["games"]` → `board game cafe`; `["active"]` → `indoor climbing gym` (a SIBLING of the injected "climbing gym", which is the prompt's intended latitude working); and in every one of those the slot came back `confident: true` with the "what kind of thing?" question GONE — the payoff, since per the prompt's own resolve rule a preference that directly answers an open aspect settles it. The override case is clean: "bowling tonight" with an art profile planned a `bowling alley` and nothing else. The mixed case is the per-aspect rule intact — art + cozy + Japanese on "a fun afternoon out" gave `art gallery` + `cafe` + `japanese restaurant` with `aesthetic: "cozy"`, three dimensions each filling their own aspect. Baseline with no profile stayed on the vague path (`entertainment district` + `bar`, `confident: false`, "what kind of thing are you in the mood for?"). **THE PHRASES ARE PLACE KINDS, AND THAT IS A SAFETY PROPERTY, NOT A STYLE CHOICE.** art→"art gallery", outdoors→"park", games→"board game cafe", active→"climbing gym", music→"live music venue". A phrase that named a FEATURE would collide with a real constraint: "live music" is provable from a provider boolean and is a genuine thing to ask for, "live music venue" is a kind of place and can never be one. A test pins that no activity phrase equals any of the strings `constraintEvidence` can emit. **THE CONSTRAINT-LEAK GUARD IS THE SECOND THING THIS STAGE GUARANTEES IN CODE RATHER THAN IN WORDING**, and it is the same bug, and the same fix, the swap engine already carries for a leaked price word and a leaked category. `constraints` is ONE plan-level array applied to EVERY slot: spliced verbatim into every query by `buildQuery`, and proved in `selectVenues` from six provider booleans and nothing else. A kind of place is unprovable BY CONSTRUCTION, so a leaked "art gallery" makes `placeMeetsAllConstraints` false for every candidate in every pool and refuses the entire plan with unmet_constraint — over a preference the user never restated. The planner path had only prompt wording, and CLAUDE.md already records the equivalent dietary wording leaking on a live run. So: firmer wording ("A PREFERENCE IS NEVER A CONSTRAINT", with the reason spelled out) AND `isLeakedActivityConstraint` + `stripLeakedPreferenceConstraints`, applied in the route beside `applyTimeFloors` because it is the same class of thing — a floor over an answer the model does not get to be wrong about. **AN EQUALITY TEST, NOT A KEYWORD LIST**, normalised with `normalizeConstraint` — the SAME function the judge normalises with — copying the category strip's reasoning exactly: "live music" is CONTAINED IN the injected "live music venue" and is also a real request, so anything looser than equality would start deleting what users actually said. Known residual, stated rather than papered over: a model leaking a VARIANT ("art" for "art gallery") is not caught, and a looser rule would cost more than it saves. The strip returns the SAME OBJECT when nothing was removed, so the no-preference path is untouched rather than cloned. One log field: `strippedConstraints`, a COUNT, emitted only when non-zero — the tuning signal for a guard whose other half is wording, and it names nobody's taste. **PART 2 ASKS FIVE OPTIONS AND NOT ONE MORE.** art / outdoors / games / active / music, each mapping to a place kind that searches cleanly, carries a sensible length and is neither weather- nor schedule-broken. Theatre and shows, classes and workshops, nightlife, shopping and spas were considered and deliberately left out: a theatre or a class is a SHOWTIME product and this app has no showtimes (the same gap `durations.ts` already records for movie runtimes), and nightlife pulls against the variety caps the planner applies to an un-named day. Collecting an answer nothing can act on is how a profile fills with dead weight. There is no "Other" either — it would name no kind and could only ever be dropped. **"cultural" WAS RETIRED FROM `style`**, which is now pure vibe (chill/lively/trendy/cozy/adventurous): it was an interest wearing a vibe's clothes, and leaving it would have steered the same choice through both the query adjective and the activity kind.
