A failed factual verdict excludes that ID and reroutes the complete chain; exhaustion fails honestly. The final schedule must have the exact affected count, valid locations, and starts byte-aligned to the computed arrivals before the engine copies the clone back once. `rerouted:false` is now a no-op persistence outcome, so even the version remains unchanged; independent GET owns status ratcheting. Added 12 required atomic/arrival cases plus a route-level no-version-bump case; the original 9 reroute invariant tests stay green. REVERT-RUNS: (1) validating all slots at the anchor failed 6/12 arrival cases; (2) accepting a missing coordinate made only the missing-middle case fail (11/12) by committing the malformed chain; restored result is 12/12. Full gate: lint exit 0 with the same 15 deferred warnings, TypeScript clean, 286/286 unit tests across 23 suites, Next 16 production build clean, and 41/41 offline mock E2E green on port 3100. Port 3000 remained untouched.

## Audit fix — Batch C: versioned persistence and atomic itinerary mutations
Goal: prevent concurrent reads, swaps, and reroutes from silently overwriting each other or refreshing Redis expiry, while preserving the locked-stop ratchet.
What should be done: give every itinerary a monotonic version; build each mutation on an isolated proposal; atomically compare-and-set in Redis and memory; preserve Redis TTL; return a deterministic conflict for stale clients; safely retry only operations that can be rerun; expose the version/ETag; prove the required swap/reroute/status interleavings and failed-write isolation.
What was done (technical): new plans start at version 1 and all loads return isolated objects. Redis mutations now execute one Lua `EVAL`: decode the current record, treat legacy unversioned records as version 1, compare the expected version, then `SET ... KEEPTTL` the complete version+1 proposal; there is no GET-then-SET CAS. Memory mode performs the same compare/set without an await between them. `updateItinerary` owns bounded load→clone→propose→CAS retries: versionless legacy operations and the pure status ratchet may retry up to three times, while an explicit client version gets one attempt and a stable 409 before any provider work if stale. GET folds lock/status ratcheting into this CAS path only when values changed, so polling neither writes nor extends expiry. Create, GET, swap, and reroute expose the version and quoted ETag; the client sends its current version on mutations. Added six interleaving/isolation cases, two route-contract cases, and one Redis TTL/CAS case. REVERT-RUN: temporarily removing the in-memory version comparison failed 4/6 CAS tests—both interleavings lost version/edits, lock-vs-swap lost a fact, and the stale writer no longer rejected; restoring it returned 6/6. Full gate: lint exit 0 with the same 15 deferred warnings, TypeScript clean, 273/273 unit tests across 22 suites, Next 16 production build clean, and 41/41 offline mock E2E green on port 3100. Port 3000 remained untouched.

## Audit fix — Batch B: bounded, validated, deadline-aware public APIs
Goal: close the audit's public-boundary denial-of-service, provider-hang, weak-validation, and sensitive-error findings without changing the planner's semantic/factual separation or its keep-on-missing behavior.
What should be done: reject oversized or malformed requests before expensive work; apply burst limits before body parsing, provider calls, or persistence; strictly validate route and model payloads; give Groq, Google, and Redis bounded deadlines; isolate partial provider failures; return stable correlated errors without leaking prompts, provider bodies, keys, or environment values; document the production controls that cannot be completed in source.
What was done (technical): added shared request, schema, provider, and logging boundaries. Every public handler now establishes a sanitized request ID, applies a route-specific in-process burst limit, enforces a 256 KB JSON ceiling, validates bounded strings/arrays/coordinates/dwell/ISO instants/IANA zones, and returns a sparse structured error with `x-request-id`; parse and swap additionally validate exact model output shapes. All Groq, Places, Routes, Weather, and Upstash REST calls now use service-specific abort deadlines and validate response envelopes without exposing upstream bodies. Places' late-night sibling searches use `allSettled`, preserving successful candidates when one query fails; the objective filter still keeps venues whose factual data is missing. Structured engine logs retain scheduling/reroute diagnostics but exclude raw prompts, refinements, model output, upstream messages, secrets, and environment values. `DEPLOY.md` now has the manual go-live checklist for shared edge/Redis rate limiting, key restrictions and quotas, Upstash scoping/TLS, Vercel environment separation, CSP rollout, WAF/bot controls, log retention/redaction, and post-deploy probes. Added nine boundary tests and provider/parse/search/swap regressions. REVERT-RUN: restoring all-or-nothing late-night search fails the new partial-sibling case (14/15); restored result is 15/15. Full gate: lint exit 0 with the same 15 deferred cleanup warnings, TypeScript clean, 264/264 unit tests across 20 suites, Next 16 production build clean, and Playwright's 41/41 offline mock E2E run recorded `status: passed` with no failures. The source limiter is deliberately labeled defense in depth: a shared production limiter remains a dashboard/infrastructure action.

## Audit fix — Batch A2: browser-offline maps fallback and locally bundled fonts
Goal: make the deterministic mock browser suite independent of Google Maps JavaScript and Google Fonts without weakening the strip↔map↔store consistency check.
What should be done: keep the production itinerary usable when Maps fails, allow a later load to recover from a cached rejection, serve the existing typefaces locally under a proper license, and prove the browser suite needs no non-local network.
What was done (technical): replaced the runtime Google Fonts import with exact OFL-1.1 `@fontsource-variable` Fraunces and Space Grotesk packages bundled by Next. The real `ItineraryMap` now catches initial and route-layer loader failures, uses a shared retryable promise that evicts rejected attempts, exposes two bounded retry attempts, and renders the same interactive venue chips on a deterministic coordinate projection over an honest fallback canvas while Maps is unavailable. The fallback is an alert, the map label now derives from the plan's actual home label rather than hard-coded Ossington, and the strip remains the primary surface. Mock Playwright deliberately supplies no browser Maps key and every spec now uses a fixture that aborts all non-local HTTP(S) browser requests; live config explicitly opts out. The existing smoke scenario now requires the fallback alert and still compares every strip time with its fallback map pin. Added three loader tests for concurrent deduplication, rejection eviction/retry, and successful cache reuse across mounts. REVERT-RUN: removing rejection eviction fails exactly the recovery case (2/3); restoring it returns 3/3. Full gate: lint exit 0 with the same 15 deferred cleanup warnings, TypeScript clean, 254/254 unit tests across 19 suites, Next 16 production build clean, and 41/41 mock E2E green with browser external traffic blocked. M8 is only partially closed here: Batch G will add browser scenarios for an invalid key, successful retry, and remount recovery while it addresses the shared client-fetch failure paths.

## Audit fix — Batch A1: supported framework and reproducible local gates
Goal: remediate the high-severity production dependency advisories and replace the interactive, partially undeclared validation workflow before changing application behavior.
What should be done: move to a supported current Next.js/React toolchain, migrate framework contracts deliberately, make lint and aggregate unit tests deterministic from declared dependencies, prove a clean install, and keep the mock E2E server isolated from port 3000.
What was done (technical): upgraded Next.js 14.2.35 to 16.2.11 and React/ReactDOM to 19.2.8; migrated the three dynamic itinerary route handlers to Next 16's asynchronous `params`; configured the repository as the Turbopack root; accepted Next's generated TypeScript route declarations and JSX setting; pinned patched PostCSS 8.5.23 and Sharp 0.35.3 only within Next's dependency tree; added ESLint 9 flat configuration, declared `tsx`, an aggregate fail-fast unit runner, explicit `lint`/`typecheck`/`test:unit`/`check` scripts, and ignored TypeScript build metadata. Two source edits are lint-only `let`→`const` corrections. A normal clean `npm ci` succeeded. The clean-install `npm run check` passed lint (15 pre-existing warnings retained for Batch J), typecheck, 251/251 unit tests across 18 suites, a Next 16 production build, and 41/41 mock E2E tests on port 3100; port 3000 was untouched. `npm audit --omit=dev` is clean. The remaining full-audit findings are dev-only transitive `brace-expansion`/`minimatch` advisories in the ESLint toolchain and are recorded rather than suppressed. Runtime Google Maps and font independence remains the separate Batch A2 application-behavior slice.

> ## RUN SUMMARY — mentor-feedback fixes (autonomous, 5 phases)
> **All five phases fully succeeded.** Committed locally in order (one commit per phase, NOT pushed, NOT deployed): `46686e3` Phase 1, `255dda4` Phase 2, `592597e` Phase 3, `24a404e` Phase 4, Phase 5 this commit. Final cross-phase run: tsc clean, 165 unit tests green across 15 suites (zoneTime + timeLabels also run under TZ=UTC/Tokyo as remote-viewer simulations), 17/17 mock e2e. Port 3000 untouched throughout; all live verification on :3200. A follow-up commit (test + live verification only, no source change) then closed two Phase-5 gaps — see the "Follow-up (Phase 5)" entry below.
> **Where I diverged from or exceeded the original ask** (details in each phase's entry):
> 1. Phase 1's actual root cause was NOT in the resolver at all — labels rendered in the viewer's browser timezone (noon Toronto = "tomorrow, 12:00 AM" for a UTC+8 viewer). The resolver was already correct, which is why the three prior guess-fixes failed. **Deployment action item for you:** nothing records that `TZ=America/Toronto` was verified on Vercel post-deploy; the `[schedule-resolve]` log prints the server TZ on every plan — check the Vercel function logs after your next deploy. (Phase 5 makes this far less critical — scheduling no longer depends on the server's TZ — but a wrong server TZ can still skew the "now" reference by its offset, so still worth the one-time check.)
> 2. Phase 2: "later today" maps to the existing "evening" day-part rather than a new now+3h rule; the new e2e answers "later today" instead of Skip (skip would be flaky 11 PM–8 AM); the Phase-1-flagged band conflict was resolved for the explicit-"now" case only.
> 3. Phase 3: parks also needed a scheduler band (6–22) — discovered live, not in the original spec; deliberately NO 14:00 park default (would roll afternoon requests to tomorrow); the requested "select prefers park" unit test is live-verified instead (a stubbed model proves nothing about the prompt rule).
> 4. Phase 4: geocoding REUSES the Places key via /api/geocode (allowed by the spec; no new env var — .env.example/DEPLOY.md updated to say so); mock e2e for a second city skipped in favour of unit tests + live Vancouver verification (mockGeocode is city-agnostic by design — flagged as follow-up).
> 5. Phase 5: **RESOLVED Phase 4's known limitation — every city now schedules and displays in its OWN timezone.** New deps tz-lookup (offline lat/lng→IANA, ~150KB — geo-tz rejected at 72MB) + luxon (zone math, over extending the ad-hoc Intl parsing that caused prior bugs); justified by size/robustness in the phase entry. Byte-compat method: default zone Toronto everywhere, verified per-file on the Toronto dev machine after each change. One real multi-city bug found and fixed beyond formatting: timeChange's absolute "to 6pm" set the SERVER's wall hour, not the plan zone's (now pinned by its own regression test, and editing — swap/time-swap/reroute — live-verified on a Vancouver plan; see the Follow-up entry). One near-miss the byte-compat bar caught: destructured `day` (day-of-month) instead of `weekday` for the hours target — the exact day-of-week corruption the spec warned about. NEW residual limitation (recorded in CLAUDE.md open gaps): the dev-only ?now= picker interprets its datetime-local value in the VIEWER's browser zone, so simulating time on a non-Toronto plan from a Toronto browser is offset for the dev control only — status derivation stays correct (absolute instants), and mock e2e is unaffected (mock plans are Toronto).
> CLAUDE.md updated where phases changed standing policy: the location-plumbing line, the mock seam count (8), the time/label conventions (now per-plan zone), and the open-gaps list.

## Feature: transit transfer bubbles — every ride of a leg, as stacked mini-circles in real line colours
Goal: `extractTransitSummary()` returned on the FIRST step with `transitDetails` — `TravelLeg.transit` was documented as "first transit ride of the leg", and every transfer after it was discarded before the data ever reached the UI. A two-transfer crosstown journey rendered as if it were one ride on one line. Data-layer change first, then the confirmed stacked-mini-circle design on the strip and the map.
What was done (technical): FIELD-MASK QUESTION SETTLED BY PROBE, NOT ASSUMPTION — the brief suspected `routes.legs.steps.transitDetails` already returns the full nested object; two live computeRoutes probes confirmed it: TTC Line 1 came back `color #f2c10b, textColor #000000, vehicle SUBWAY`, and a Liberty-Village→Scarborough route returned TWO ordered segments (63 Ossington #ed1c24 BUS → Line 2 #009247 SUBWAY). No mask change anywhere. DATA LAYER — `extractTransitSegments()` walks EVERY step in riding order; `TransitSummary` gained `shortName` (the agency's own designation, the bubble label), `color`, `textColor`, `vehicle` — all null when unpublished, keep-on-missing. COMPAT DECISION, as the brief asked me to call: `TravelLeg.transit` is KEPT, defined as exactly `transitSegments[0]` — persisted KV itineraries already carry it, and `fireDisruption`'s line-name lookup plus `legDetail` read it; changing its type would force a store migration for zero gain. New `transitSegments?` carries the full ordered array; new readers prefer it, and BOTH client mapping sites fall back to `[transit]` for pre-segments stored plans (a one-element stack renders identically to the old single badge, so old plans lose nothing). GROUPING — the confirmed design lives in `app/lib/transitBubbles.ts`, PURE and shared by strip and map so the two can't drift: consecutive pairs of small stacked circles, an odd leftover as one full-size circle LAST (1 ride → one full circle, today's badge as the n=1 case; 2 → one pair; 3 → pair + single; 4 → two pairs), plus `bubbleLabel` (shortName when published, initials from the line name otherwise — "Lakeshore West" → "LW" — never blank). STRIP — the bubble stack replaces the generic transit glyph when segments exist (walk/unknown/segment-less transit keep their glyphs); each bubble is the line's real colour with its short designation, full name in the tooltip. The leg's text line is now HONEST on multi-ride legs: "2 transfers · 82 min" instead of the FIRST ride's line name and stop count, which would misread as the whole journey's (single-ride legs keep the exact old text — the "505 Fixture" e2e pin never moved). MAP — the same treatment at map scale on the `.leglab` midpoint pills, with one stated simplification: paired small circles are colour-only dots (a 9px circle cannot carry text); full-size singles keep the short name; `legDetail`'s label text gets the same transfers-honesty rule. MOCK — `mockLeg` mirrors the extraction contract (`transit === transitSegments[0]`, one ride) with fixture colour data; the fixture layer supplies DATA, the grouping is LOGIC and runs for real in mock e2e. TESTS — new `transitBubbles.test.ts` (18th suite) pins the grouping EXACTLY per the brief's five cases (0/1/2/3/4 rides, plus 5, plus order preservation) and the label rules; travel.test.ts gained a MULTI-RIDE extraction case (bus→subway→streetcar with walk steps interleaved: all three survive in order, colours carried, the third ride's unpublished colour stays null, `transit` deep-equals `transitSegments[0]`) and the existing single-ride pin was updated to the richer shape (a deliberate pin move, noted here). One test-fixture touch-up in reroute.test.ts (its hand-built TransitSummary needed the four new fields — tsc caught it). REVERT-RUN: with travel.ts stashed, exactly the two extraction tests fail (14/16), nothing else. VISUAL VERIFICATION, same process as the re-skin: a throwaway `/bubbles-specimen` route rendered the strip at 0/1/2/3/4 segments and the map pills at 1–4, screenshotted, then deleted before commit. LIVE VERIFICATION on :3200 with real Routes data: a Scarborough-Town-Centre start against a Chinatown ramen stop produced a genuine THREE-RIDE home leg — 129 bus (red) / Line 2 (green) stacked pair + full-size 510B streetcar (red), "2 transfers · 82 min", riding order correct, and the map midpoint pill showing the dot-pair + "510B" + the same honest text. FLAGGED IN PASSING, not fixed (out of scope): "jazz bar" draws the "What kind of bar?" narrowing question — the bar rule's specific guard has no jazz/live-music term; one-line guard addition for a future round. Verified: 251 unit tests across 18 suites green, tsc clean, 41/41 mock e2e.

## Feature: themed prompts expand into a real multi-stop day — and "a full schedule" is time information
Goal: reported — "plan a full schedule for things to do as a soccer fan tomorrow" produced ONE stop, when the expectation was a themed sequence (stadium/landmark, then a soccer-adjacent museum stop, then food). Root cause confirmed live before building, exactly as the brief suspected: the real parse returned `category_signals: ["soccer"]`, `stop_count: null` — the parse prompt captures "every distinct activity", but a themed request doesn't LIST activities, it states an interest, and nothing told the model to expand one into several.
What was done (technical): PRE-BUILD FINDING that shaped the fix — `stop_count` is parsed, typed, and threaded but consumed NOWHERE in the pipeline (grep: no reader outside parse/type/test fixtures); the stop count is driven purely by `category_signals` length. So the brief's "infer stop_count 3-4" would have produced dead data, and loosening its carefully-pinned rule ("never count listed activities yourself") risked regressing "dinner then a bar" → null for zero behavioural gain. DEVIATION, documented: `stop_count`'s rule is untouched; the expansion count rides on the categories themselves. (1) THEME EXPANSION — the recommended prompt-rule approach held up under live testing, so the clarify fallback fork was NOT taken: the parse `category_signals` rule now recognizes interest/identity phrasing ("as a ___ fan", "a full day around ___", "explore ___ culture", "immerse myself in ___") combined with an open-ended ask and expands it into 3-4 DISTINCT, map-searchable facet categories (worked example in the prompt: soccer → stadium tour / sports museum / sports bar / restaurant), with guards: never four near-synonyms, at most ONE general food stop, and NO expansion when the user names a concrete kind ("a soccer museum" → exactly that) or lists their own activities. Live probes (temp 0): the reported prompt → ["soccer stadium tour", "sports museum", "sports bar", "restaurant"]; "history buff" → museum/landmark/archaeological site/historical society; "foodie saturday" → restaurant/cafe/bakery/food market; over-trigger guards clean ("a soccer museum" → one category; "dinner then a bar at 8" → two, unexpanded; "ramen tonight" → one). (2) THE SCHEDULING INTERPLAY the expansion exposed — tracing the expanded set through the resolver: with time "tomorrow" and no day-part, the earliest-category anchor let the FOOD facet's 19:00 default set the start, so a "full day" began at 7 PM and the museum facet arrived after close (Group C's arrival-time filter would empty its pool → recovery panel). The honest reading: "a full schedule"/"a full day" IS time information — duration language the parse discarded. The time_window rule now captures it as exactly "all day" (appended to day qualifiers: "tomorrow, all day"), and `DAY_PART_DEFAULTS` gained an "all day" entry. THE HOUR IS 11:00, NOT 10:00, DELIBERATELY: the plausibility check requires the start inside at least one matched band, a themed day usually carries a food facet, and both food bands (bar 11→2, restaurant 11→23) open at 11 — a 10:00 anchor would have REFUSED the exact plan this feature builds, while 11:00 sits on the bands' opening edge. Listed last in the table so specific day-parts win when both appear. (3) THE DETERMINISTIC FLOOR — same judgment as the immediacy round: live probing showed the model captures "all day" for direct phrasings but returned "unspecified" for "immerse myself in japanese culture for a day" even after a prompt nudge ("for a day" reads as part of the immersion phrase). A missed signal degrades to a 7 PM start for a DAY ask — wrong tone, cascading empties — so `app/lib/allDayTime.ts` (sibling of immediateTime.ts, same contract) floors it from the RAW prompt: strong phrases only (all day / a full/whole/entire day/schedule / full schedule / for a/the day), bare "day" deliberately unmatched ("day trip ideas some other day" must not anchor at 11). The floor APPENDS rather than replaces — a model-captured "tomorrow" survives as "tomorrow, all day", an explicit clock time still wins downstream (clock branch precedes day-parts), and it composes INSIDE the immediacy floor ("right now … for a full day out" → "now": immediacy outranks duration). Applied to both parse branches (mock + real) — logic, not data. (4) COMPOSITION with the generic-category axis, checked not assumed: expanded facets flow through `genericCategoryQuestion` — "sports bar" passes its own specific guard, bare "restaurant" legitimately draws "What are you craving?" (the food stop of a themed day is exactly the case that question exists for; one tap). END-TO-END LIVE on :3200, the brief's key test: the exact reported prompt → the cuisine question (answered "Italian") → a FOUR-stop plan for TOMORROW starting 11:00 AM — Rogers Centre stadium tour 11:23, Hockey Hall of Fame 12:52 (museum OPEN at arrival — the 7-PM-start version died right here), The Draft Room sports bar 3:39, La Vecchia ITALIAN RESTAURANT 5:41 (the narrow answer folded into the facet). Honest note: for a SOCCER fan, Toronto's Places data surfaced a baseball stadium and a hockey museum — facet RELEVANCE within a category is the select step's existing territory (BMO Field ranks below Rogers Centre for "stadium tour"; Toronto has no soccer museum), and the parse layer's job (distinct facets, right kinds) was done correctly. NO mock e2e added, same precedent as Phase 3's select-prompt rule: theme expansion is MODEL behaviour — a fixture that fakes the expansion would test the fake; live verification stands in. The floors ARE unit-tested (mock-model worst cases). TESTS: +1 parse case (floor stamps "all day" when the model loses it; append-not-replace preserves "tomorrow"; no double-append when captured; immediacy wins when both; never fires on bare "day"s) and +1 schedule case ("tomorrow, all day" + the real four-facet set → tomorrow 11:00, checked resolver PASSES — pinning the 11-not-10 choice — and "tonight, all day" → tonight 20:00). REVERT-RUNS: floor stashed → exactly the parse ALL-DAY case fails (5/6); day-part entry stashed → exactly the schedule ALL-DAY case fails (36/37). Verified: 243 unit tests across 17 suites green, tsc clean, 41/41 mock e2e.

## Fix: "tomorrow" survives the last fallback — a day qualifier can no longer be silently dropped
Goal: found by direct code reading, then live-confirmed — `resolveStartTime` computes `dayOffset` once at the top and threads it into the clock-time, day-part, and category-inference branches, but the FINAL fallback (`return nextFullHourInZone(now, timeZone)`) never took it. So "plan a full schedule for things to do as a soccer fan tomorrow" typed at 11:38 PM — no clock time, no day-part keyword, and "soccer" matching nothing in `CATEGORY_START_DEFAULTS` — fell all the way through and discarded "tomorrow" entirely: the plan resolved at the next full hour (midnight), and the default plausibility band (which deliberately wraps 8→1 AM for late-night city prompts) let the overnight plan sail through. This is the reported bug's mechanism.
What was done (technical): LIVE-VERIFIED FIRST, as always — the real parse for the exact reported prompt returned `time_window: "tomorrow"`, `category_signals: ["soccer"]`, so the parse was NOT the problem here; the trace lands precisely on the fallback line. FIX SHAPE — a documented deviation from the brief's suggestion ("next full hour + a day-add"): that shape fails its own test at the reported repro time. At 11:38 PM the next full hour is ALREADY midnight — tomorrow's date — so a day-add overshoots to the day AFTER tomorrow; and at 10 PM it produces "tomorrow 11 PM", which is the same overnight complaint moved one day later (and for park-like categories lands outside their band → a refusal for a perfectly plannable request). The hour the user happened to TYPE at is an artifact, not a request — projecting it onto another day means nothing. Instead: "tomorrow" with no other time info means "tomorrow, daytime" — the fallback now anchors at `DAY_PART_DEFAULTS.morning` (10:00) on the offset day via the existing `instantAtWallClock`, exactly where "tomorrow morning" already lands, making the rule explainable in one sentence. No `zoneTime.ts` addition needed (the brief asked to check — `instantAtWallClock` already covers it). The `dayOffset === 0` path is byte-identical (next full hour), and the explicit-"now" branch still returns BEFORE day math — "now" keeps ignoring day qualifiers, correct on purpose per the brief. TESTS: +3 in schedule.test.ts — (1) the repro: "tomorrow" + "soccer" at 11:38 PM → tomorrow 10:00, never tonight, plus the afternoon-typed variant ("tomorrow" + "axe throwing" at 13:20 → tomorrow 10:00, not today 14:00); (2) regression: "tomorrow" + a table-matched category ("dinner") still takes the category default on the right day (that path already worked); (3) "now, tomorrow" → today's next full hour (the immediate branch wins before day math). REVERT-RUN PROVEN: with the fix stashed, exactly the repro test fails (35/36), nothing else moves. Verified: 241 unit tests across 17 suites green, tsc clean, 41/41 mock e2e.

## Fix: "right now" means TONIGHT — a deterministic immediacy floor, late-night search breadth, and an honest all-empty message
Goal: reported live at 11:28 PM — "restaurants to eat at right now" came back scheduled for TOMORROW at 8 instead of tonight, and the empty-tonight case hid behind the generic "everything nearby got filtered out. Try a different time?". Three fixes, each verified before trusting the hypothesis.
What was done (technical): (1) THE TIME BUG — hypothesis verified live before fixing, with a twist: the raw parse for the reported prompt returned time_window "unspecified", NOT the suspected "tonight" — the immediacy was lost ENTIRELY. Chain confirmed: unspecified → restaurant's 19:00 category default → already passed at 11:28 → rolled forward a full day. `resolveStartTime`'s immediate branch was already correct; it just never received "now". Fixed at both layers the brief suggested, and BOTH kept (judgment call): the parse system prompt now teaches the model that immediacy phrasing ("right now", "ASAP", "as soon as possible", "at this moment", "open now") is time information captured as exactly "now" — that covers the long tail ("as soon as we can head out") a regex can't. And a deterministic floor (`app/lib/immediateTime.ts`, same spirit as swap.ts's parseTimeExpr: time language is never trusted to the LLM alone) scans the RAW prompt and stamps time_window "now" regardless of what the model returned. The floor is applied in the parse ROUTE to BOTH branches — mock and real — because it is LOGIC, not data, and the mock seam must not fork around it (which also makes the whole repro exercisable in mock e2e). LINE DRAWN: strong phrases only (right now / right away / ASAP / a.s.a.p. / as soon as possible / immediately / at this moment / this instant / open now); bare "now" is deliberately NOT matched on raw prompt text ("now that I think about it…"). Live-verified on :3200 against the real model: all three probe phrasings now parse to "now". At 11:28 PM the request now resolves to TONIGHT (midnight next-full-hour) and the band check refuses honestly — which beats silently booking tomorrow. (2) LATE-NIGHT SEARCH BREADTH — premise probed before building, real Places data, Toronto 23:30: plain "restaurant" returned 6/20 open and 14/20 closed — the same well-known-but-shut skew GENERAL_QUERIES fixed for the vague pool (its live evidence was 15/20). The "late night restaurant" variant surfaced 8/20 open with partial overlap, so `searchPools` gained `opts.lateNight`: each named category also runs its "late night <category>" variant and unions the results (primary first, deduped — roughly doubles the genuinely-open pool). The places route computes the flag from the ALREADY-resolved instant's local hour in the plan's zone (>= 21 or < 5); swap/reroute call searchPools directly and don't pass the flag yet — scope kept contained, noted as a follow-up. (3) THE HONEST MESSAGE — precisely as located in the brief: `emptyCategoryReason` (partial case) was already honest; `noVenuesReason` (all-empty, the path the reported case hits) never saw the drop log. It now takes it, and rather than a second copy of the reason logic, `reasonFromDrops` was refactored into shared `dominantRule` + `phraseForRule` helpers (one copy of the strings): hours-dominant → "Couldn't find any restaurants spots still open around 12:00 AM — everything nearby is closed at that hour. Try a different time, or a different kind of place?" (general across categories, not restaurant-coded); other dominants (price/rating/…) reuse their existing per-rule phrasing; NO drop data → the original generic message byte-identical (the "beach" e2e pin is untouched). TESTS: +1 parse case proving the floor independent of the LLM (model deliberately answers "tonight" — the worst case — and three variants incl. "whats open now"; a stated "7pm" passes through untouched); +1 schedule case pinning "now"→TONIGHT 22:00 at 21:15 AND the buggy "unspecified"→tomorrow-19:00 contrast AND the 11:28 PM midnight resolution; +4 planGuards cases (hours-dominant message, price/rating keep their own phrasing and never borrow the late-night line, no-drops byte-identical, partial path unchanged); +1 searchPools case (lateNight → exactly two queries with the union deduped primary-first, daytime → one query byte-identical); +1 e2e ("drinks right away", client frozen 21:15: the narrowing question shows WITHOUT a When? re-ask — the floor already answered it — and the plan lands "be here 10:xx PM" with no "tomorrow"; server-hour-proof via the hours-less Night Owl fixture). REVERT-RUN PROVEN at both levels: disabling the floor fails exactly the e2e (the "tomorrow" label — the reporter's symptom) and the parse unit case, nothing else. Verified: 238 unit tests across 17 suites green, tsc clean, 41/41 mock e2e.

## Feature: a GENERIC category draws its own narrowing question — presence is not specificity
Goal: "restaurant tonight" skipped clarification entirely — category present, time present, done — even though a bare "restaurant" is nowhere near enough to search well. The existing kind question only fires when there's NO category at all; there was no equivalent for "you gave me a category, but a broad one."
What was done (technical): `clarify.ts` gained a second axis — category SPECIFICITY — as a `GENERIC_RULES` table: each rule pairs a generic pattern (bare restaurant/dinner/lunch/food; something-to-do/entertainment/activity; bare bar/drinks; bare dessert; bare shopping) with a SPECIFIC guard (cuisines/dishes; named activities; bar sub-types; dessert sub-types; shop kinds), a question, options, and a fold-back mode. `genericCategoryQuestion(category, constraints)` returns the narrowing question or null — the guard checks the category AND the constraints, so "restaurant" with a "vegan" constraint isn't re-asked (the constraint already shapes the query). THE SKIP CONDITION IS CHANGED PRECISELY, NOT LOOSENED: `hasCategory && (hasTime || hasAesthetic)` still skips — the sole new exception is `narrowQuestions.length > 0`, so every specific-category prompt behaves byte-identically to before. Budget: narrow questions share the existing 3-question cap, sit ABOVE when/vibe (narrowing WHAT beats both), one question per DISTINCT category ("drinks then another bar" = two slots, one question, one answer applied to both). ANSWER FLOW — folded into the CATEGORY STRING, not constraints, and that choice is deliberate: constraints are HARD requirements enforced with unmet-constraint refusals, and a cuisine preference must narrow the search, not arm a refusal when no venue description happens to evidence "Italian". Two modes per rule: cuisines are PREFIX modifiers ("Italian" + "restaurant" → "Italian restaurant" — still matches the restaurant duration, the dinner band, and builds "Italian restaurant Ossington Toronto" through the existing buildQuery), venue-type answers REPLACE ("bar" → "cocktail bar", "entertainment" → "arcade"). New `applyNarrowAnswer` does the fold; `page.tsx` gained `clarifyNarrow` state (a Record keyed by category — several narrow questions can coexist), chips + free-text per question, and the submit fold-back. LINE DRAWN (documented as asked): the axis applies to categories present AT PARSE TIME. Answering the kind question with "something to do" still deliberately lands on the general pool with no second clarify round — the flow stays one round, and "surprise me" stays surprise-me. MOCK CONTRACT: `mockParse` now keeps sushi/ramen as their own signals instead of collapsing them to "dinner" — mirroring the real parse rule ("ramen stays ramen") that this feature depends on; without it the "sushi tonight asks nothing" case would be untestable in mock. TESTS: clarify suite 10 → 14 (each family asks; specific members and constraint-narrowed categories don't; fold-back both modes; budget caps at 3 with vibe falling off first; duplicates ask once) — three OLD pins updated because they pinned the exact behavior this changes (their skip cases now use "sushi"/"ramen"/"bowling", keeping the unchanged-rule assertion honest); +2 e2e ("restaurant tonight" asks the cuisine question WITHOUT re-asking When, answer lands an "Italian dinner" plan on Velvet Fig; "sushi tonight" plans straight through with no clarify). E2E FALLOUT, expected and intended: recovery-flow specs type prompts like "…then a bar at 7pm", and bare "bar"/"dinner" now draw their narrowing question first — those specs skip through it via `dismissClarifyIfShown`, which was HARDENED in passing: it clicked `.clarify__skip` by class, and the recovery panel's "Plan without it" shares that class — one layout change away from silently dropping a stop mid-test; it now targets the "Skip — just plan it" button by accessible name. Verified: 231 unit tests across 17 suites green, tsc clean, 40/40 mock e2e.

## Design: soft-blue re-skin (palette only — zero feature or logic changes)
Goal: take the design LANGUAGE from the two original first-draft mockups (`Canvas.pdf`, `Itinerary Landing.pdf`) — not their layout or feature set — and replace the warm-paper/ink-navy theme with the pale-blue/teal one. Explicitly a re-skin: nothing under `app/api/**` or `app/lib/**` was touched, no behaviour, copy, validation or test assertion changed.
What was done (technical): PALETTE — `globals.css`'s token VALUES were replaced while the token NAMES (`--paper`, `--card`, `--ink`, `--rule`…) were deliberately kept, so every existing rule kept working and the diff stayed a re-skin rather than a rewrite. New: `--accent` #5BB3CC (icon badges, buttons, focus), `--accent-deep` for hover, `--label` #4E9FBA for small-caps labels, a four-stop `--hero-*` sky ramp, and two shared shadow tokens. `--ink` became a desaturated navy #1E3A4C, `--card` became white (the mockups' card surface), `--paper` a hazy pale blue. THE RESERVED ACID-GREEN IS UNCHANGED — `--live` is still exactly #C8F000 and still exclusive to "happening now / just changed" (the `NOW` pill, the active marker, the changed-stop border, the settling time pill). It was checked against the new backgrounds rather than assumed: it reads as a harder pop against cool blue than it did against warm paper, so no shade adjustment was needed. Typography untouched — Fraunces + Space Grotesk, same scale. HERO — the empty state is now a full-bleed sky (pale at top → deeper teal at the foot) with the horizon rendered as a very wide flat ellipse, a blurred vertical reflection band beneath it, and a radial glow behind the wordmark; all three are decorative `pointer-events: none` layers. The wordmark is the white Fraunces "Itinerary" with the app's own existing tagline "life moves simpler." (already in `layout.tsx`'s metadata) italic beneath. SEARCH PILL — the three separate controls (prompt input, city, start address) became ONE white capsule with three labelled sections, hairline dividers and a circular accent button overlapping the trailing edge. Same three pieces of state, same validation (`busy || !prompt.trim() || !city.trim()`), same Enter-to-submit, same `runPipeline` trigger — purely presentational regrouping. Because the go button became an icon, the loading text it used to display moved to a small `.empty__status` pill under the pill. The slim top bar that the hero recedes into got the same treatment in miniature (capsule, accent button). CARDS — stop cards took the mockup's language: white surface, 16px corners, a rounded-square accent badge, a teal small-caps category label, a bold headline, a star row, and an italic description. The badge carries the STOP NUMBER rather than a generic glyph — same visual language, more information. MAP — the style JSON went from warm greys to cool pale blues with the POI/transit-label stripping rules byte-identical, and the route lines from ink-navy to a deep teal (#2E6F8A) with the dashed-transit / solid-walk distinction and leg labels unchanged. Recovery panel, clarify block, banner, weather chip, swap bar, loading pill and the dev strip were all recoloured with copy and behaviour untouched.
PHOTO-THUMBNAIL DECISION (flagged as requested): the mockup's destination cards carry a decorative postcard image; **no image panel was added, in any form**. The app has no venue photo data — Places' field mask doesn't request photos, and fetching them would be a data/API change, not a design one — and a flat gradient stand-in would cost real width on a 182px card sitting in a horizontal strip over a live map, for purely decorative gain. The accent badge, star row and italic description carry the visual interest instead. Review counts are omitted for the same reason: the mockup shows "(218 reviews)", but `userRatingCount` isn't in the field mask either, so the star row shows stars + the numeric rating the pipeline already has.
TWO PRE-EXISTING FINDINGS, surfaced not silently fixed: (1) `.chip--selected` is applied by `ItineraryMap.tsx` and its own comment says the chip is "highlighted when selected" — but NO CSS rule ever defined that highlight, so selecting a stop changed nothing on the map. Added as part of the card restyle (accent border + lift, matching the selected strip card). (2) the `.ecard*` block (~20 lines) is DEAD: the expanded editorial card was replaced by the compact chip and no component renders it today. Restyled for consistency rather than deleted — removing it is a cleanup decision, not a re-skin one, so it is flagged here for you rather than actioned.
PROCESS: a throwaway `/specimen` route rendered the tokens on representative pieces (hero, pill, stop cards, chips) and was screenshotted for review before any rollout, matching the "design plan approved as a first pass" step the original UI build used; it and a temporary screenshot spec were deleted before commit. Verified: 227 unit tests across 17 suites green, tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e — and NOT ONE e2e assertion needed changing, because every existing class name was preserved and only its styling replaced (`.prompt__input` in particular was kept unique to the search field, since the helpers locate it strictly). Port 3000 untouched; screenshots taken on :3200 and the mock server.

## Fix: opening hours travel with the stop — the adapt step can finally fire (closes the Group A gap)
Goal: the gap flagged during Group A's work and recorded in its DEVLOG entry — NOT a finding in `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` itself, though §1.1 is the background. `placeOf()` reconstructs a `Place` from a stored stop for the availability seam, but `ItineraryStop` carried no `currentOpeningHours`, so the field was ALWAYS undefined, `isOpenAtInstant` correctly hit keep-on-missing, and `usableByHours` answered "usable" for every stored stop unconditionally. Net effect: the documented try → adapt → notify ladder's ADAPT step had never once fired against real data. It appeared to work only in mock mode, where `fixtureHoursById` patched the hours back in by venue id — a fixture that was quietly MORE capable than production, papering over the exact gap it should have exposed.
What was done (technical): hours now travel with the pick exactly as `priceLevel`/`description`/`rating` already do, for the same stated reason (a later reader must not depend on a pools lookup that has gone stale — here it wasn't even stale, it was absent). `Selection` gained `currentOpeningHours?: CurrentOpeningHours`, populated at BOTH sites that build a Selection from a Place (the fresh-pick path and the highest-rated fallback), one line each next to the existing carried fields. `SelectionLike` gained the same field, so it types through to `ScheduledStop` → `ItineraryStop`. `buildSchedule` needed NO code change: both its push sites already spread `...sel`, so the field rides along the moment the type admits it — worth stating explicitly rather than adding a redundant line. `buildStop` threads it on both branches (`picked.pick.currentOpeningHours` / `kept!.currentOpeningHours`), which after Group I covers `finalize` and `venueSwap` together. `placeOf()` passes `stop.currentOpeningHours` through — that one line is the actual fix. TYPE IMPORT NOTE (small deviation from the brief): `CurrentOpeningHours` is declared in `hours.ts` and `filter.ts` imports it without re-exporting, so `selectVenues.ts` takes it from the canonical source rather than from `filter.ts` as the brief suggested. MOCK CLEANUP — and it went further than removal. `fixtureHoursById` was PROVEN dead before deleting it, not assumed: `mockSelect` was first taught to carry hours like the real `selectVenues` (without that, mock stops would still arrive hours-less and the gap would persist in mock mode), then the lookup was made to THROW and the full e2e suite run — 38/38 still passed, so nothing reaches it. A `console.warn` probe was tried first and rejected as a weak signal, since server-side logs don't surface in Playwright's stdout. With the lookup gone, `mockIsUsableAt` reduced to exactly `usableByHours`, so instead of keeping a duplicate the mock deps now take the REAL function by injection (`mockSwapDeps(parseTime, parseDuration, isUsableAt)`, injected rather than imported to avoid the swap↔fixtures cycle, same pattern as the deterministic parsers). Mock and production can no longer disagree about what "open then" means, by construction — the fixture layer supplies DATA, the engine supplies LOGIC. `fixtures.ts` now imports only the hours TYPE. TESTS: +3 in `swap.test.ts`, all building through the real `buildSchedule` → `createItinerary` → `placeOf()` chain with NO injected `isUsableAt`. (1) THE ONE THAT MATTERS: a 9-to-5 museum downstream of a dinner that gets pushed 3 hours later — the ladder must ADAPT it away. REVERT-RUN PROVEN: with `placeOf`'s single line removed, exactly this test fails with "closed downstream venue should have been adapted away, not kept" (47/48) and nothing else moves. (2) A carrier test: hours survive select → schedule → a JSON store round-trip (as KV would do) → `placeOf`'s reconstruction, verified by asking the seam directly at an open and a shut instant. (3) KEEP-ON-MISSING regression: a venue with no hours data at all stays `undefined` through the whole chain (never coerced to something falsy-but-defined) and is KEPT rather than adapted — correctly unaffected by the revert-run, since it's about absent hours. Verified: 227 unit tests across 17 suites green (swap also under `TZ=UTC`), tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group I: one traits table, two shared helpers, one stop builder (closes §5.3, §5.4, §5.5)
Goal: `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` §5's remaining duplication. §5.3 — FOUR regexes answered overlapping questions about the same category and disagreed: `filter.ts`'s `OUTDOOR_PATTERN` (weather gate), `searchPlaces.ts`'s `PARK_PATTERN` (Places type filter), `durations.ts`'s park rule, and `schedule.ts`'s park band. A category containing "patio" was weather-gated but got no park type filter, park duration, or park band; "bench" was the exact inverse — hard-filtered to Places' park type yet never weather-gated, so a bench sit could be planned in a thunderstorm. §5.4 — the timed-index loop and the legs rebuild were each copy-pasted four times. §5.5 — `finalize` re-derived stop construction, the duration-preservation rule, and the buffer clamp inline instead of calling `buildStop`.
What was done (technical): new `app/lib/categoryTraits.ts` is the single answer to "what KIND of thing is this category", exposing TWO deliberately distinct traits rather than one boolean — `parkLike` (green space: park search type, park duration, dawn-to-dusk band) and `outdoor` (weather-gated: park-like OR open-air). That distinction is the point: a patio bar is weather-exposed but is not a park and must not be searched, timed, or banded like one. All four sites now consult it — `filter.ts` re-exports `isOutdoorCategory` from it, `includedTypeFor` asks `isParkLike` (its casino rule stays a local regex, since that's genuinely unrelated), `resolveCategory` resolves park via the traits table in the position its local rule occupied, and the plausible-bands table takes a small `{ test }` adapter so the table keeps its uniform shape while membership comes from one place. The union deliberately keeps `\bwalk\b` as a word boundary, which FIXES a real inconsistency in passing: `durations.ts` used a bare `walk` substring, so a "boardwalk cafe" was given a park's 40-minute duration; it is now a cafe on every axis. §5.4 `timedIndexes(itinerary)` and `rebuildLegs(itinerary)` live in `store.ts` (where the Itinerary shape is defined) and replace all eight copies. §5.5 `finalize` now calls `buildStop` with a `durationOverride`, deleting a parallel 15-field object literal and a second copy of the buffer clamp — a future field on `ItineraryStop` no longer has to be remembered in two places, which is the bug shape that produced the `priceLevel`/`description`-on-the-stop rule in the first place. TESTS: +1 cross-pipeline coherence case asserting the four axes AGREE for the categories they used to disagree on — "bench" and "green space" now get the park type filter, the weather gate, the park duration AND the park band (with a 23:30 bench sit correctly refused); "patio" is weather-gated but gets none of the park treatment; "boardwalk cafe" is a cafe everywhere; and an ordinary "park walk" is unchanged on every axis. THE TEST EARNED ITS KEEP IMMEDIATELY: it failed on first run because the bands-table rewiring hadn't actually applied (the adapter was declared but the old regex was still in the table), so "bench" was falling through to the default 8-to-1 band — exactly the kind of half-applied consolidation that leaves the codebase looking unified while behaving as it did before. Verified: 224 unit tests across 17 suites green, tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group H: meridiem reads the category, engines read the weather (closes §7.3, §7.6; §7.4/§7.5 closed earlier)
Goal: `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` §7.3 — `parseTimeExpr`'s bare-hour PM assumption was applied without reference to the stop, so "make it 10" on a BRUNCH stop became 22:00 and was then refused by the plausible-band guard with "A 10:00 PM brunch won't work": an honest report of a decision that was itself wrong. §7.6 — both engines passed `weather: null` to `filterPools`, so only the initial plan ever consulted the forecast and a swap or reroute could move an outdoor stop into the rain undetected.
What was done (technical): §7.3 `parseTimeExpr` takes an optional `category` and, when that category has a known band, prefers the reading that fits it: AM is kept ONLY when the AM hour is inside the band and the PM hour is outside. Everything else is untouched — no category, or a category whose band admits the evening reading, still gets the outing-planner PM default, and an explicit meridiem always wins outright. The membership test is shared rather than re-derived: `schedule.ts` now exports a pure `hourInBand(h, band)` that its own zone-aware `inBand` also calls, so the swap engine's bare-hour reasoning and the scheduler's instant-based check can't drift. `interpretRefinement` threads the stop's category into the deterministic floor. §7.6 a new `app/api/weather/fetchWeather.ts` holds the server-side forecast fetch, and both engines gained a `getWeather` dep (injectable like every other pipeline dep, so the guarantee stays testable without network) defaulting to it, keyed on the itinerary's own `home` coordinates. `reroute`'s TODO is gone — the replan now filters against the real forecast anchored at `floor` — and swap's `venueSwap` and `findReplacement` do the same. Keep-on-missing is preserved end to end: no key, no coords, a failed lookup, or a pre-multi-city plan with no `home` all yield `null`, which skips the gate exactly as before. The mock deps return the fixture forecast, so mock e2e exercises the same path. NOTE ON THE OTHER TWO §7 ITEMS: §7.4 (`withStatuses` could never return "planning") landed in Group F, because it is a rewrite of the same status expression §2.4 touches; §7.5 (`parseTargetTime` dropping a stated time that isn't the first number) landed in Group A, because that group already rewrites that function's signature. Both are noted in their own entries; nothing from §7 is outstanding. TESTS: +1 swap case for the meridiem (no category → 22:00 unchanged; brunch/breakfast keep AM; dinner and bar still take the PM default so the heuristic isn't weakened; an explicit am/pm overrides the band either way); +1 swap case for §7.6 (a 90%-rain forecast blocks an outdoor pool so the swap refuses honestly, and the identical swap succeeds with calm weather). Verified: 223 unit tests across 17 suites green, tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group G: failures degrade instead of destroying the plan (closes §6.1, §6.2, §6.3, §6.4)
Goal: `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` §6 — four places where a plausible failure was handled worse than its siblings. §6.1 `Promise.all` across per-category searches meant one category's rate-limit threw away every OTHER category's good results as a 500. §6.2 when both routing modes failed, the leg became `{mode:"unknown", totalMinutes:0}` — "we don't know" scheduled as "zero minutes", i.e. the next stop the instant this one ends, across any distance, rendered by the strip as a confident "walk · 0 min". §6.3 a schema-valid-but-wrong LLM answer surfaced a backend contract string to the user. §6.4 a failed refresh returned silently, leaving the strip and map showing state the store no longer agreed with.
What was done (technical): §6.1 `searchPools` uses `Promise.allSettled`; a failed category becomes an EMPTY pool plus a `DropEntry` with a new `searchFailed` rule (and its own honest phrasing in `reasonFromDrops`: "the venue search didn't come back for this one — worth trying again"), which routes it straight into the recovery panel — the flow that already exists for exactly this shape. Only a TOTAL wipeout still throws. Failures ride out through an optional `{ failures }` out-param so the signature stays compatible for swap/reroute, and the places route prepends them to `_dropLog`. §6.2 the estimate is computed in `getSingleLeg`, not `buildLeg` — the pure function keeps its honest zero, and the caller (which has the coordinates) fills in a conservative straight-line walking estimate at 5 km/h, keeping `mode: "unknown"` so the number is never presented as a promise; the strip gained an explicit unknown branch reading "travel time unavailable / ~N min (estimated)" instead of rendering it through the walk branch. §6.3 the parse route now NORMALIZES the model's answer — every documented field coerced to its documented empty value (`location: ""`, `category_signals: []`, mixed-junk arrays filtered, wrong types replaced) — so a shape miss degrades into a vague-but-plannable prompt instead of a developer-facing error two routes later; an outright unparseable answer now returns `UNPARSEABLE_MESSAGE` (the planGuards text) with the technical detail alongside, and the select route's `SelectParseError` likewise answers "Couldn't pick venues for that just now — try again?" with `detail`/`raw` kept for debugging. §6.4 `refreshItinerary` surfaces both a thrown fetch and a non-OK response ("what you see may be out of date"), and a failed store now says the plan is shown but can't be swapped or rerouted, rather than leaving controls that quietly do nothing. TESTS: +2 searchPlaces cases (one category rate-limits → the good category still returns, the failed one is an empty pool with a `searchFailed` drop entry carrying the real message; and a total wipeout still throws); +1 travel case (both modes fail over ~4.5 km → mode stays "unknown" but `totalMinutes` is a positive ~53-minute estimate, never 0, with the distance reported so the UI can qualify it — the pure `buildLeg` unknown-is-0 test still passes unchanged, which is the point); +a new `parse.test.ts` suite (4 cases: a missing `location` still yields a usable parse, junk field types are coerced rather than passed through, unparseable output surfaces the fail-loud message plus detail, and a well-formed answer passes through byte-identical). Verified: 221 unit tests across 17 suites green, tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group F: store invariants tightened (closes §2.1, §2.2, §2.3, §2.4; §7.4 landed early)
Goal: `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` §2 — four ways the store's own rules could be bypassed. §2.1 `createItinerary` wrote straight into the in-memory Map, making it the ONE entry point that "succeeds" on Vercel without KV configured (and leaving a shadow copy that is never read once KV is on). §2.2 `getItinerary` was an exported, unused, synchronous bypass of the KV seam — it would work perfectly in dev and return `undefined` for every request in production. §2.3 `finalize` used `continue` where `resettleTail` uses `break` for the same "never move a locked stop" condition. §2.4 every GET wrote back, so a polled read cost a Redis round trip and refreshed the TTL, meaning an actively-viewed plan never expired.
What was done (technical): `createItinerary` is now a pure factory — build and return, no `store.set`; `saveItinerary` is the only write path and the only one that enforces `requirePersistenceOnServerless`. Every existing caller already paired the two, so nothing else moved. `getItinerary` is DELETED rather than renamed: it had no callers anywhere, and its signature was more convenient than the correct one (`loadItinerary` is async), which is exactly what made it dangerous. `finalize`'s loop now breaks at the first locked/past stop, matching `resettleTail`; the ratchet always held, but skipping over a locked stop kept shifting the ones beyond it, so an earlier stop could be pushed into the locked stop's slot. §2.4: `withStatuses` takes an optional `{ changed }` out-param (non-breaking — the two engines ignore the return entirely), and the GET route saves ONLY when something actually moved. SCOPE NOTE (documented deviation, same pattern as §7.5 in Group A): §7.4 from Group H — `withStatuses` could never return `"planning"`, because the first GET overwrote it — was fixed HERE, since it is a rewrite of the very status expression §2.4 also touches, and doing them separately meant editing the same three lines twice. The new `nextItineraryStatus` returns `"planning"` until some stop is active or completed. Group H will note it as already closed. TESTS: +1 itinerary case for the dirty flag (a plan booked for tomorrow can be polled all day with `changed: false` — no write, no TTL refresh — while moving into a stop reports true); +1 proving `createItinerary` does NOT persist (`loadItinerary` returns undefined until saved); +1 swap case for §2.3 with a locked stop BETWEEN the swapped stop and a later one — REVERT-RUN PROVEN (restoring `continue` fails exactly that case, 42/43), and it needed a genuinely overflowing swap to discriminate: my first attempt used a same-length replacement, so the reflow loop never ran and the test passed against the bug. TESTS THAT PINNED THE BUG: two `itinerary.test.ts` cases asserted `itinerary: "active"` for a plan where nothing had started — the §7.4 symptom — and now assert `"planning"`. Verified: 214 unit tests across 16 suites green, tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group E: the last four `any`s are gone (closes §4.1, §4.2, §4.3, §4.4)
Goal: `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` §4 — four `any`s in production code, three of them the SAME object: the parse result, which is mutated in place (`.city =`, `.home =`) and threaded through the entire client pipeline (geocode → time check → places → select → store) untyped for all of it.
What was done (technical): one type applied at all three sites — `PlanCtx.parseData`, the `time-gate` recovery arm's `parsed`, and `continuePipeline`'s parameter are now `ParsedPrompt`, which was already sufficient because `city` and `home` are declared optional on it (that's how the app-injected fields ride along). Two adjacent holders came with it — `parsedObj` state and `clarify.parsed`/`submitClarify`'s `updated` were `Record<string, unknown>`, which would have blocked the change — so the parse is now one type from `/api/parse` to the store. §4.1: a local `RawForecastHour` interface in the weather route describes the parts of Google's payload actually read, the same "declare the shape you consume" pattern `travel.ts` uses for `ComputeRoutesResponse`. THAT ONE PAID FOR ITSELF IMMEDIATELY: with the real shape in place, `tsc` rejected the mapping — `hourISO` was typed `string` on `WeatherHour` but produced as `string | null`. The `.filter()` below already dropped those hours at runtime, so the code was correct by accident; it is now a proper type GUARD (`h is WeatherHour`), which is what actually makes the nullable field safe. A latent inconsistency that `any` had hidden for the life of the file. Verified with the audit's own check: `grep` for `: any` / `as any` / `<any>` / `any[]` across `app/` excluding tests returns NOTHING. 211 unit tests across 16 suites green, tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group D: no invented locations, one fallback, an honest DEPLOY note (closes §3.1, §3.2, §3.4, §5.1)
Goal: `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` §3.1/§5.1 — `FALLBACK_PARSED` was defined twice, verbatim, in `reroute.ts` and `swap.ts`, both hardcoding `location: "Ossington"` with no city. For an itinerary stored without its `parsed` field, a Vancouver plan's swap or reroute would search Toronto's west end and compute travel legs to venues 3,000 km away. §3.2 — the ambient weather chip fetched with no coordinates, falling back to the route's Ossington anchor. §3.4 — `DEPLOY.md` justified its `TZ` requirement with a rationale that is no longer true.
What was done (technical): one shared `fallbackParsedFor(itinerary)` in a new `app/api/itinerary/fallbackParsed.ts`, consumed by both engines. It invents NO neighbourhood (`location: ""` searches the city broadly rather than pretending the plan was in Ossington), and — the part that can't be faked — it REFUSES rather than guess a city: `buildQuery` falls back to Toronto when `parsed.city` is absent, which is correct only for plans that really are Toronto, so the fallback returns null when the itinerary's resolved zone is anything other than `America/Toronto` and both engines answer with an honest `UNKNOWN_LOCATION_MESSAGE`. That keeps legacy pre-multi-city plans (no zone, or Toronto) working exactly as before while making a knowably-non-Toronto plan fail loud instead of silently searching the wrong city — the project's stated posture everywhere else. §3.2: the parameterless ambient fetch on mount is GONE rather than relabelled. It was only ever needed for the chip, the chip renders only on the map stage, and by then the plan's own coordinate-scoped forecast is already in state — so the fix removes the fabricated-location call entirely instead of dressing it up. The route keeps `DEFAULT_LOC` for old clients, now documented as not-relied-upon. §3.4: `DEPLOY.md`'s serverless section rewritten to say plainly that the ORIGINAL reason is stale (scheduling is per-plan zone-aware now and genuinely doesn't read `TZ`) while the CONCLUSION still holds for the narrower reason the audit found, with an explicit "don't remove it just because the old explanation is stale" — the exact failure mode of a half-corrected doc. The env table's "REQUIRED, see above" became "recommended; see the note above for what it does and does NOT do now". TESTS (the audit noted neither fallback branch had ANY coverage — both engines' tests always supply `parsed`): +2 swap cases — a Toronto plan with `parsed` deleted still swaps and searches with `location: ""` (no invented neighbourhood), and a Vancouver plan with `parsed` deleted refuses with the honest message, never calls `searchPools` at all, and leaves the stop untouched. Verified: 211 unit tests across 16 suites green, tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group C: the plan stops pretending everything happens at once (closes §1.4, §1.5, §1.7)
Goal: `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` §1.4 — the objective filter judges EVERY category at the plan's single anchor instant, because per-stop arrival times don't exist yet when it runs. So "dinner and drinks and dessert" filtered the dessert pool on *open at 7pm* and shipped Sundown Scoops (closes 21:00) for a stop you reach at 22:17 — a plan that cannot be executed, with nothing downstream re-checking it. §1.5 — every travel leg was routed with the OUTING's start time, so a late leg got early-evening transit frequencies. §1.7 — a recovered slot re-resolved its own start time from its one category, landing on a different instant than the slot it was filling.
What was done (technical): DEVIATION FROM THE AUDIT'S PROPOSED SHAPE, documented as instructed. The brief said to route any stop failing the arrival re-check into the recovery panel. Implemented as written, that fired on `dinner and drinks and dessert` — a completely ordinary prompt — and four existing e2e specs stalled on a recovery panel, which is the correct diagnosis but the wrong response: it makes an interruption the norm and hands the user a problem the app can solve itself. This project's documented ladder is try → ADAPT → notify (it is exactly what `resettleTail` does for swaps), so `finishPipeline` now follows the same one: TRY the plan, and if a stop's own venue is shut at its own `start_time`, ADAPT by picking the best-rated venue in the ALREADY-FETCHED pool that is genuinely open then (no extra API call), re-running the route+schedule once because new venues move the times; only if the pool has nothing open at that hour does it NOTIFY via the existing recovery panel. The adapt is announced in the banner ("Swapped in Midnight Flour — the first pick would have been closed by the time you got there"), never silent. `finishPipeline` was restructured around an inner `planOnce(sels)` so the two passes share one code path; the escalation empties the failing slots first, so "Plan without it" drops them rather than shipping a closed venue AND the re-check terminates instead of re-opening the panel forever (`skipArrivalCheck` makes that termination explicit rather than incidental). §1.5: `getTravelLegs` takes `dwellMinutes[]` and accumulates a per-leg departure (start + each preceding stay + each leg already priced), passing each leg its own instant; `getSingleLeg` already accepted one, so only the caller changed. This makes leg fetching SEQUENTIAL — leg i+1's departure isn't knowable until leg i is priced — which at 2–4 legs is a few hundred ms for a schedule that's actually right; documented in the function's comment. The travel route and `page.tsx` thread `dwellMinutes` from the dry-run schedule. §1.7: `filterPools` already accepted a `targetOverride` (it's how reroute anchors at floor) — it just wasn't reachable over HTTP, so the places route now accepts `targetTime` and uses it for both the filter and the `[schedule-resolve]` log; `PlanCtx` carries the plan's resolved `startInstant` and `searchSlot` sends it, so widen/replace/weather-override all filter against the instant of the slot they're filling. TESTS: +1 travel case pinning that leg 1 departs 115 minutes after leg 0 (10 min transit + 5 margin + a 105-minute dinner) and that the two legs never share a departure time; +1 searchPlaces case proving the route honours an explicit `targetTime` (Sundown Scoops survives a 7pm anchor and is correctly filtered out at an explicit 10pm target, so the route used the instant it was GIVEN rather than re-deriving one); +2 e2e (a venue closing before arrival is adapted to Midnight Flour with the banner saying so and Sundown Scoops absent; and a general assertion that EVERY scheduled stop starts before its venue's closing time). FIXTURE-ENCODED-BUG NOTE: the existing "description line" spec asserted Sundown Scoops renders on `dinner and drinks and dessert` — i.e. it pinned the buggy plan. Its actual subject is the description-less card, so it now states a 5pm start, where all three fixture picks are genuinely open on arrival; the late case became the new adapt test rather than being deleted. Verified: 209 unit tests across 16 suites green, tsc clean, 38/38 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group B: a repeated category is two stops, not one (closes §7.1, §7.2)
Goal: `code-audit-2026-07-18.md` §7.1 — candidate pools are keyed by category STRING, so `["bar","bar"]` ("a drink, then another drink somewhere else") collapsed to one key, one selection, one stop. The user asked for two places and silently got one: nothing came back empty, so no fail-loud guard and no recovery panel ever saw it — the second stop simply never existed. In reroute it was worse: two affected stops sharing a category both resolved to the SAME `Selection` object, planning one venue twice in an evening. §7.2 — the UI identified a stop by its category string, so with duplicates, clicking or swapping the second card always acted on the first.
What was done (technical): DEVIATION FROM THE AUDIT'S PROPOSED SHAPE, documented as instructed. The audit suggested keying pools positionally (`bar#0`, `bar#1`). On closer inspection that synthetic key leaks into everything that legitimately needs the HUMAN category — `getDuration`, the plausible bands, `isOutdoorCategory`, the drop-log detail strings, `emptyCategoryReason`'s user-facing text, `orderByRequest`, the strip's eyebrow label — and, fatally, into the recovery flow's `searchSlot`, which addresses pools by bare category over HTTP. So pools stay keyed by category and the SLOT bookkeeping moved into selection, which is where "one venue per requested stop" actually belongs. Same outcome, far smaller blast radius. `searchPools` now de-duplicates categories before searching (the duplicate request was making two identical Places calls whose second result overwrote the first — so this is also a cost fix). `selectVenues(apiKey, parsed, pools, slots?)` takes the requested stops in order, duplicates intact: the model is given a `slots: [{slot, category}]` list, must answer one entry per slot, and must never repeat a venue across slots; `findProblems` validates per slot and treats a repeated id as an error that triggers the existing correction retry; assembly walks slots in order with a `taken` set, and the highest-rated fallback now picks the best UNUSED candidate. `indexBySlot` is deliberately tolerant — a model answering by category only (the pre-slot contract) still lines up, so a shape regression degrades to old behaviour instead of failing every slot into fallback. NARROWING IS ANNOUNCED, never silent: when a category has fewer distinct venues than slots asked for, the extra slot comes back `id: null, narrowed: true`, and new `narrowedSlotReason` gives it its own honest recovery row ("You asked for more than one bar, but I could only find one nearby — want to look further out, or try something different for the second stop?") rather than the empty-pool wording, which would be a lie (the venue exists; it's already in the plan). Reroute drops the `byCategory` map for slot indexing (with the same tolerant fallback for slot-less dep implementations). §7.2: stop identity is now the VENUE ID end to end — `page.tsx`'s `selected` state, `doSwap`'s `findIndex`, `styledStops`' status join, `selectedStop`, and both view components (`ItineraryMap`, `ItineraryStrip`) — which the audit explicitly allowed as an alternative to a positional key, and which is simpler since every rendered stop already has a unique non-null id. The recovery panel needed the same treatment: rows gained an optional `slot`, with `rowKey`/`matchesRow` helpers, so resolving a narrowed duplicate no longer overwrites its filled twin (`mergeSlot` matches the row's slot when known); `partialEmptySelections` returns the selections themselves, with `partialEmptyCategories` kept as a thin view for the pre-slot callers and tests. `mockSelect` mirrors the whole slot contract including distinctness and narrowing — the fixture-fidelity lesson from the `mockSelect` ordering bug: a fixture that collapsed duplicates would make mock e2e agree with the very bug being fixed. TESTS (the audit confirmed nothing anywhere used a duplicate category): +3 select cases (two slots → two distinct venues and the model is actually told about both; a repeated venue triggers the retry with "already used by an earlier slot"; one venue for two slots → `narrowed: true`, never dropped), +1 reroute case (two affected bar stops get different venues; REVERT-RUN PROVEN — restoring the `byCategory` map fails exactly that case, 8/9), +1 searchPools case (a repeated category costs ONE API call, pool keyed once), +1 mock fixture trigger (`another bar|two bars|bar hop|second bar` duplicates the drinks signal, documented in `e2e/README.md`), and +2 e2e (a repeated category plans two stops with different venues — Ten O'Clock Curfew then The Standing Room, both eyebrows "drinks"; and selecting the SECOND duplicate card opens ITS swap prompt and reason, not the first card's). Verified: 207 unit tests across 16 suites green, tsc clean, 36/36 mock e2e.

## Audit fix — Group A: one zone-aware openness check (closes §1.1, §1.2, §1.3, §1.6, §5.2; §7.5 landed early)
Goal: the foundational fix from `code-audit-2026-07-18.md`. "Is this venue open at instant X" existed in two-and-a-half divergent copies — one correct (the objective filter, via `wallClockParts`) and two reading the SERVER's wall clock (the swap engine's availability default, and the mock fixture that shadows it). Collapse them into one shared, zone-aware helper, and remove the dead server-local `day` field that made the same mistake easy to reintroduce.
What was done (technical): `hours.ts` gained the single entry point — `targetTimeAt(instant, timeZone)` (THE instant→venue-local `TargetTime` conversion) and `isOpenAtInstant(hours, instant, timeZone)` = `isOpenAt ∘ targetTimeAt`, keeping the tri-state keep-on-missing contract. All three call sites now go through it: `filter.ts` builds its target with `targetTimeAt` (behaviour identical — it was the correct one), `swap.ts`'s `usableByHours` and `fixtures.ts`'s `mockIsUsableAt` delegate, the fixture keeping ONLY its id→hours lookup. §1.1's zone is threaded explicitly through the seam: `SwapDeps.isUsableAt` gained a 4th `timeZone` parameter rather than closing over it in `realDeps()`, so every call site (`timeChange`, `resettleTail`, `findReplacement`) passes the plan's zone visibly and existing test stubs — which take fewer params — still compile. §1.2: `parseTargetTime` now returns a new `ClockTime {hour, minute}` and takes no `now` — the dead `day` field is gone, and with it the trap of a correctly-shaped, server-computed value sitting on a type whose other consumer treats `day` as load-bearing (resolveStartTime already owned "tomorrow" and roll-forward, so behaviour is unchanged). §1.6: `buildSchedule`'s three `cursor.setMinutes(cursor.getMinutes() + n)` sites became millisecond addition — stop durations are elapsed time, and local-field math normalizes against the server's clock, so it shifted by an hour across the SERVER's DST transition inside a function whose contract is per-plan zone correctness. SCOPE NOTE (documented deviation): §7.5 (Group H) — `parseTargetTime` taking the first number and giving up if it's a bare duration, silently dropping the "7pm" in `"2 hours, 7pm"` — was fixed HERE rather than in Group H, because Group A rewrites that same function's signature and doing both meant one rewrite instead of two stacked ones; it now scans all numeric candidates and takes the first with a meridiem or colon. Group H will note it as already closed. TESTS — the audit's coverage note was the point: every one of the 38 existing swap cases injects its own `isUsableAt`, so the production default had ZERO coverage. New `hours.test.ts` suite (8 cases) pins the shared helper on explicit absolute instants so it holds under any runner TZ, incl. both halves of the bug (16:30 Vancouver = OPEN while 19:30 Toronto / 23:30 UTC = closed; and Mon 20:30 Vancouver being already Tuesday in UTC), keep-on-missing through the wrapper, and `isOpenAtInstant ≡ isOpenAt ∘ targetTimeAt` across four zones. Two new swap cases exercise the REAL default with no stub — one calling exported `usableByHours` directly with a Vancouver zone, one running `swapStop` with `isUsableAt` deliberately omitted from the injected deps so `realDeps()` supplies it (pins the wiring the stubbed cases structurally cannot see). REVERT-RUN PROVEN: with `usableByHours` restored to `when.getDay()/getHours()`, exactly those two new cases fail (38/40) and nothing else moves. Verified: 202 unit tests across 16 suites green (was 192/15), tsc clean, 34/34 mock e2e; the new suites also pass under `TZ=UTC`.
FINDING THE AUDIT MISSED — flagged, not fixed (out of this group's scope, and not in the audit): **the availability seam is effectively inert in production today.** `placeOf(stop)` (swap.ts) builds a `Place` from a stored stop, and stored stops carry no `currentOpeningHours` — so `usableByHours` hits keep-on-missing and returns TRUE for every stored stop, always. Both callers that could reject (`timeChange`'s anchor check, `resettleTail`'s per-stop check) feed it exactly such an object, and `findReplacement` — the only place a candidate WITH real hours is tested — is reachable only from those two. Net effect: the documented try → adapt → notify ladder never adapts in production; it only fires in mock mode, where `fixtureHoursById` supplies the hours the real path lacks. That also means §1.1's severity in production was lower than the audit stated (the wrong-zone verdict was masked by never being consulted) — but the underlying gap is arguably larger, and it is exactly the kind of thing mock-only exercise hides. The Group A fix stands on its own (the helper is now correct and the trap is gone); whether to give the seam real hours data — carry `currentOpeningHours` onto stored stops, or re-fetch in `placeOf` — is a product call for you, so it is recorded here and NOT silently implemented.

## Docs + audit: README/CLAUDE.md refreshed to the shipped feature set; full logic audit run (report only)
Goal: both documents had drifted behind what actually ships (multi-city + per-city timezones, the three-mode recovery panel, the late-hour and weather gates, distance-aware selection/swaps, the broken-transit walk escape hatch), and no systematic logic review had been done since that work landed. Two deliverables in one pass: bring the docs current, then audit the codebase with fresh eyes. NO application code was touched — the audit is a report to be triaged into batches later, not a batch itself.
What should be done: refresh README (live feature list, every env var the code actually reads, limitations reconciled against CLAUDE.md's open gaps) and CLAUDE.md (open gaps re-verified item by item, plus any section whose shape no longer matches the code); separately produce a dated logic-only audit with file+line, impact, severity, and a test-coverage note per finding.
What was done (technical): DOCS — README rewritten in place (same voice/structure, not a from-scratch rewrite): the Ossington-only framing is gone, a "What it actually does" section now covers multi-city zones, distance-aware picks, the recovery panel's three situations, and the walk-relabel rule; the env section was rebuilt from an actual `grep` of `process.env.*` rather than memory, adding a second table for `KV_REST_API_URL`/`KV_REST_API_TOKEN` (+ the `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_*` aliases), `VERCEL`, `E2E_MOCK`, and `TZ` — with each one's reading module named, and the note that there is NO Geocoding API key (geocode reuses the Places key); "Known limitations" is now an explicit short mirror of CLAUDE.md's gap list so the two documents can't contradict each other. CLAUDE.md — "What this is" de-Ossingtoned; a new "Shipped — interactive recovery panel" operational section records the union's three modes and their load-bearing rules (overridable set ONLY on the inferred path; weather override = `weather:null` and nothing else; "something else" scopes to the slot, not the plan); the fail-loud guard-order line now says where the panel sits and when a refusal string is the wrong answer; "Currently in progress" replaced (it still described pre-deploy state and 16/16 e2e). OPEN GAPS re-verified item by item against code rather than trusted as written — most stand; three changed: the weather item was NARROWED (the panel closed the "no recourse" half; the residual "a declined block has no pin" is still true and kept), "reroute skips weather gate" was CORRECTED to "reroute AND swap" (both pass `weather: null`), and the standing `TZ` note was corrected — server TZ no longer drives scheduling, but it does still leak in via the swap engine's `usableByHours`, which is an audit finding, not a policy.
